    *   **Causality:** Although the paper demonstrated some correlation between the hyper-parameters and performance, the causal relationship is not well explained or studied.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future model architecture design and training strategies for MoE-based LLMs, particularly in contexts where reasoning is a critical requirement. It highlights the need to move beyond simplistic scaling laws and consider the interplay between architectural choices, hyperparameters, and task-specific optimization.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign this paper a score of **7**. It represents a solid and valuable contribution, but it's not a groundbreaking or paradigm-shifting work.

*   **7 is justified because:**
    *   The paper has clear *novelty* in its focused investigation of MoE sparsity and its careful handling of the many performance measures.
    *   The findings are practically *significant*, providing helpful design guidelines.
    *   The *strengths* are substantial, including systematic experimentation and rigorous analysis.
    *   The *weaknesses*, while present, don't invalidate the findings, but they do limit the scope of the conclusions.

The paper doesn't achieve a higher score because it's somewhat limited by the dataset size and model architecture, and lacks strong explanation about causal relations of the experimental results. However, it is still useful and a solid advancement to the field.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Tailored Teaching with Balanced Difficulty: Elevating Reasoning in Multimodal Chain-of-Thought via Prompt Curriculum
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18673v1)
- **Authors**: Xinglong Yang, Quan Feng, Zhongying Pan, Xiang Chen, Yu Tian, Wentong Li, Shuofei Qiao, Yuxia Geng, Xingyu Zhao, Sheng-Jun Huang
- **Abstract**: The effectiveness of Multimodal Chain-of-Thought (MCoT) prompting is often limited by the use of randomly or manually selected examples. These examples fail to account for both model-specific knowledge distributions and the intrinsic complexity of the tasks, resulting in suboptimal and unstable model performance. To address this, we propose a novel framework inspired by the pedagogical principle of "tailored teaching with balanced difficulty". We reframe prompt selection as a prompt curriculum design problem: constructing a well ordered set of training examples that align with the model's current capabilities. Our approach integrates two complementary signals: (1) model-perceived difficulty, quantified through prediction disagreement in an active learning setup, capturing what the model itself finds challenging; and (2) intrinsic sample complexity, which measures the inherent difficulty of each question-image pair independently of any model. By jointly analyzing these signals, we develop a difficulty-balanced sampling strategy that ensures the selected prompt examples are diverse across both dimensions. Extensive experiments conducted on five challenging benchmarks and multiple popular Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) demonstrate that our method yields substantial and consistent improvements and greatly reduces performance discrepancies caused by random sampling, providing a principled and robust approach for enhancing multimodal reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of effectively using Multimodal Chain-of-Thought (MCOT) prompting in Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It argues that randomly or manually selected prompt examples often lead to suboptimal performance due to a mismatch between the model's knowledge distribution and the task's complexity. To address this, the authors propose a framework called CAMS (Complexity-Guided Active Multimodal CoT Sampling). CAMS constructs a prompt curriculum tailored to each model by integrating two complementary signals: model-perceived difficulty (using prediction disagreement from active learning) and intrinsic sample complexity (measuring the inherent difficulty of question-image pairs). The framework then uses a difficulty-balanced sampling strategy to create diverse prompt examples. The authors evaluate CAMS on several challenging benchmarks and show that it yields significant improvements, reduces performance variability, and offers a more robust approach to multimodal reasoning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novelty:** The idea of using a prompt curriculum tailored to individual MLLMs based on both model-perceived difficulty and intrinsic sample complexity is novel. It moves beyond random or clustering-based prompt selection methods.
    *   **Significance:** Improving the stability and performance of MCOT prompting is significant for enhancing the reasoning capabilities of MLLMs. CAMS addresses a practical challenge that affects the usability of these models in complex multimodal tasks.
    *   **Thoroughness:** The paper includes extensive experiments on multiple benchmarks and models. The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the importance of both uncertainty analysis and complexity evaluation.
    *   **Clarity:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the CAMS framework, the experimental setup, and the results. The figures and tables are helpful in understanding the method and its performance.
    *   **Practicality:** The method is relatively easy to implement and could be readily adopted by researchers and practitioners working with MLLMs.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity Scorer Dependence:** The performance of CAMS relies on the accuracy of the complexity scorer. While the authors train a scorer based on ChatGPT ratings, its effectiveness might vary across different datasets and domains. A more robust and generalizable complexity estimation method could be explored.
    *   **Computational Overhead:** Active learning-based sampling, especially with multiple sampling iterations, can be computationally expensive. The paper could benefit from a discussion of the computational cost of CAMS compared to simpler prompt selection methods.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** The performance may be sensitive to the choice of hyperparameters, such as the number of sampling iterations (k) and the balance between easy and hard examples. More detailed guidance on how to select these parameters could be beneficial.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   CAMS could inspire future research on adaptive prompt selection and curriculum learning for MLLMs. The idea of tailoring prompt examples to individual model characteristics could be applied to other prompting techniques and tasks.
    *   The framework could be used to improve the robustness and reliability of MLLMs in real-world applications. By reducing performance variability, CAMS could make these models more trustworthy for decision-making.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to improve MCOT prompting in MLLMs. The CAMS framework is both theoretically sound and practically effective, as demonstrated by the extensive experimental results. While the reliance on a potentially domain-specific complexity scorer and the computational overhead are limitations, the overall contribution is significant. CAMS addresses a critical issue in the field and provides a valuable tool for enhancing multimodal reasoning capabilities. Therefore, a score of 8 is justified.
- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Requirements Development and Formalization for Reliable Code Generation: A Multi-Agent Vision
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18675v1)
- **Authors**: Xu Lu, Weisong Sun, Yiran Zhang, Ming Hu, Cong Tian, Zhi Jin, Yang Liu
- **Abstract**: Automated code generation has long been considered the holy grail of software engineering. The emergence of Large Language Models (LLMs) has catalyzed a revolutionary breakthrough in this area. However, existing methods that only rely on LLMs remain inadequate in the quality of generated code, offering no guarantees of satisfying practical requirements. They lack a systematic strategy for requirements development and modeling. Recently, LLM-based agents typically possess powerful abilities and play an essential role in facilitating the alignment of LLM outputs with user requirements. In this paper, we envision the first multi-agent framework for reliable code generation based on \textsc{re}quirements \textsc{de}velopment and \textsc{fo}rmalization, named \textsc{ReDeFo}. This framework incorporates three agents, highlighting their augmentation with knowledge and techniques of formal methods, into the requirements-to-code generation pipeline to strengthen quality assurance. The core of \textsc{ReDeFo} is the use of formal specifications to bridge the gap between potentially ambiguous natural language requirements and precise executable code. \textsc{ReDeFo} enables rigorous reasoning about correctness, uncovering hidden bugs, and enforcing critical properties throughout the development process. In general, our framework aims to take a promising step toward realizing the long-standing vision of reliable, auto-generated software.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces REDEFO, a novel multi-agent framework designed to enhance the reliability of automated code generation (ACG). REDEFO aims to bridge the gap between potentially ambiguous natural language requirements (NLRs) and precise, verified executable code by incorporating formal methods into the ACG pipeline. The framework consists of three specialized agents: Analyst (interprets and structures NLRs), Formalizer (translates informal specifications into formal specifications), and Coder (generates verified executable code from formal specifications). The framework leverages knowledge acquisition from real-world projects, authoritative literature, and human experts to improve agent capabilities. The paper presents a case study in the aerospace domain to demonstrate REDEFO's potential.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of integrating formal methods with a multi-agent LLM architecture for reliable ACG is relatively novel. While LLM-based code generation and the use of agents for software development are existing concepts, REDEFO's structured approach to requirements development, formalization, and verification differentiates it from existing methods. The emphasis on knowledge infusion from various sources (codebases, literature, experts) strengthens the approach. However, similar ideas like using formal methods or static analysis for code verification exists, the novelty lies in the structure.

*   **Significance:** If successful, REDEFO could significantly improve the quality and trustworthiness of automatically generated code. This is crucial for applications in safety-critical domains where correctness is paramount. The potential impact on software development productivity and the reduction of human error are considerable. Furthermore, the framework's ability to generate a high-quality corpus of aligned data (requirements, specifications, code) is a valuable contribution to the field.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The framework addresses a critical problem in LLM-based code generation: the lack of guarantees about code correctness and adherence to requirements.
    *   The multi-agent architecture provides a structured and modular approach to the complex task of requirements-to-code transformation.
    *   The incorporation of formal methods enables rigorous reasoning about correctness and the detection of subtle bugs.
    *   The emphasis on knowledge acquisition from diverse sources enhances the agents' capabilities and ensures practical relevance.
    *   The integration of human experts in the loop allows for expert judgment at critical points and more active participation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper is largely conceptual and lacks extensive empirical validation. The case study demonstrates the *potential* of REDEFO but doesn't provide definitive proof of its effectiveness in complex, real-world scenarios.
    *   The paper doesn't fully address the scalability challenges. The computational cost of formal verification and code synthesis can grow rapidly with project size and complexity.
    *   The dependence on high-quality training data for each agent is a potential bottleneck. Creating and maintaining such data can be time-consuming and expensive.
    *   The paper needs more details on the specific techniques used for NL processing, knowledge representation, and formalization (e.g., specific formalisms, rule-based vs. statistical methods).
    *   The practical challenges of deploying the framework, especially in terms of resource constraints and integration with existing development workflows, are not adequately discussed.

*   **Potential Influence:** REDEFO has the potential to influence future research in automated code generation by highlighting the importance of formal methods and structured requirements development. It could also inspire the development of new tools and techniques for knowledge infusion and agent collaboration in software engineering. The focus on reliability and correctness is particularly important as LLMs are increasingly used in critical applications.

**Justification for Score:**

A score of 6 reflects the following considerations:

*   The core idea is a solid one but builds on existing concepts. Its novelty, while present, is incremental rather than groundbreaking. The idea is not entirely new but an extension to an existing idea.

*   The lack of extensive empirical validation significantly limits the paper's impact. While the case study provides a proof of concept, more rigorous experiments are needed to demonstrate the framework's effectiveness in realistic scenarios.

*   The potential significance is high, but the practical challenges of scalability, data requirements, and deployment need to be addressed.

*   The clarity of the presentation and the well-defined framework are strengths. However, the paper could benefit from more technical details and a more in-depth discussion of the practical implementation challenges.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Utilizing Training Data to Improve LLM Reasoning for Tabular Understanding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18676v1)
- **Authors**: Chufan Gao, Jintai Chen, Jimeng Sun
- **Abstract**: Automated tabular understanding and reasoning are essential tasks for data scientists. Recently, Large language models (LLMs) have become increasingly prevalent in tabular reasoning tasks. Previous work focuses on (1) finetuning LLMs using labeled data or (2) Training-free prompting LLM agents using chain-of-thought (CoT). Finetuning offers dataset-specific learning at the cost of generalizability. Training-free prompting is highly generalizable but does not take full advantage of training data. In this paper, we propose a novel prompting-based reasoning approach, Learn then Retrieve: LRTab, which integrates the benefits of both by retrieving relevant information learned from training data. We first use prompting to obtain CoT responses over the training data. For incorrect CoTs, we prompt the LLM to predict Prompt Conditions to avoid the error, learning insights from the data. We validate the effectiveness of Prompt Conditions using validation data. Finally, at inference time, we retrieve the most relevant Prompt Conditions for additional context for table understanding. We provide comprehensive experiments on WikiTQ and Tabfact, showing that LRTab is interpretable, cost-efficient, and can outperform previous baselines in tabular reasoning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Utilizing Training Data to Improve LLM Reasoning for Tabular Understanding":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LRTab, a novel prompting-based approach designed to improve Large Language Model (LLM) reasoning for tabular understanding. LRTab aims to bridge the gap between finetuning and training-free prompting by learning from the errors made during the training process. It first generates chain-of-thought (CoT) responses on the training data. For incorrect CoTs, it prompts the LLM to predict "Prompt Conditions" that, when added to the original prompt, would correct the error. These prompt conditions are then stored and retrieved during inference, acting as context to guide the LLM towards better performance. The approach combines text similarity and a custom cross-encoder reranker to retrieve the most relevant conditions. The paper demonstrates LRTab's effectiveness on WikiTQ and TabFact datasets, achieving state-of-the-art results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of learning from mistakes in training data by generating and retrieving "Prompt Conditions" is a notable contribution. While similar approaches use training data for in-context learning, LRTab's focus on explicitly identifying and mitigating errors through prompt engineering is novel. The paper offers a middle ground between finetuning and training-free prompting, incorporating advantages from both paradigms.

*   **Significance:** The results on WikiTQ and TabFact are compelling, demonstrating that LRTab achieves state-of-the-art performance. The approach is also shown to be interpretable and cost-efficient, which are important practical considerations. The analysis of best practices for table understanding (coding vs. direct prompting, retrieval model ablations, etc.) also provides valuable insights to the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-structured explanation of the method.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on benchmark datasets.
    *   Detailed analysis of the effects of different components (prompt conditions, CoTs, encoders/rerankers).
    *   Interesting insights into the benefits of flexible prompting strategies and the importance of model capacity.
    *   Provides valuable qualitative examples that illustrate the workings of prompt condition retrieval.
    *   The paper successfully addresses the limitations of both finetuning (high cost, lack of generalizability) and inference-only prompting (lack of leveraging labeled data).

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The need for substantial initial training data can limit application to truly low-resource settings, as stated in the conclusions. The dataset bias toward initial training may also impact generalizability in certain cases.
    *   While the cost-efficiency claim at *inference* is supported, training the cross-encoder and generating prompt conditions still involves significant computational overhead. The paper states that training took approximately 2 weeks and a large model was required. There is a tradeoff here compared to direct prompting approaches and finetuning strategies.
    *   The "Prompt Conditions" are not as easily generalizable as embeddings learned in the finetuning setting. This may limit transfer learning and use in new domains.
    *   The paper doesn't fully explore the limitations of the approach, such as the cases where prompt conditions are insufficient to correct errors, or if retrieval becomes a bottleneck.
    *   Some implementation details, such as the exact prompt templates used, are only available in the Appendix.

*   **Potential Influence:** LRTab has the potential to influence the field by inspiring new prompting strategies that better leverage labeled data for tabular reasoning. The idea of learning from errors and using retrieved context to guide LLM behavior is a generalizable concept that could be applied to other tasks. The analysis of different prompting techniques and their impact on LLM performance can also inform future research.
*   **Room for Improvement:**

    *   Analyze the types of errors that prompt conditions are *unable* to fix.
    *   Explore active learning strategies to minimize the amount of data that must be initially labeled.
    *   Investigate methods for generalizing prompt conditions to new domains.
    *   Provide a detailed explanation on how the cross-encoder reranker improves retrieval.

**Justification for Score:**

LRTab presents a significant advancement in LLM prompting for tabular reasoning by effectively incorporating information from labeled data. The concept of using errors to automatically generate prompt conditions is clever and has the potential to improve both the accuracy and interpretability of LLMs. Although the approach requires a substantial amount of training data and incurs some computational overhead, it achieves state-of-the-art results and offers valuable insights into the benefits of flexible prompting strategies. While improvements could be made in terms of generalizability and robustness, LRTab represents a substantial contribution to the field.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### FALCON: Autonomous Cyber Threat Intelligence Mining with LLMs for IDS Rule Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18684v1)
- **Authors**: Shaswata Mitra, Azim Bazarov, Martin Duclos, Sudip Mittal, Aritran Piplai, Md Rayhanur Rahman, Edward Zieglar, Shahram Rahimi
- **Abstract**: Signature-based Intrusion Detection Systems (IDS) detect malicious activities by matching network or host activity against predefined rules. These rules are derived from extensive Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI), which includes attack signatures and behavioral patterns obtained through automated tools and manual threat analysis, such as sandboxing. The CTI is then transformed into actionable rules for the IDS engine, enabling real-time detection and prevention. However, the constant evolution of cyber threats necessitates frequent rule updates, which delay deployment time and weaken overall security readiness. Recent advancements in agentic systems powered by Large Language Models (LLMs) offer the potential for autonomous IDS rule generation with internal evaluation. We introduce FALCON, an autonomous agentic framework that generates deployable IDS rules from CTI data in real-time and evaluates them using built-in multi-phased validators. To demonstrate versatility, we target both network (Snort) and host-based (YARA) mediums and construct a comprehensive dataset of IDS rules with their corresponding CTIs. Our evaluations indicate FALCON excels in automatic rule generation, with an average of 95% accuracy validated by qualitative evaluation with 84% inter-rater agreement among multiple cybersecurity analysts across all metrics. These results underscore the feasibility and effectiveness of LLM-driven data mining for real-time cyber threat mitigation.
- **Summary**: Okay, let's break down this paper on FALCON and its approach to autonomous cyber threat intelligence mining with LLMs for IDS rule generation.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces FALCON, an autonomous agentic framework designed to generate and validate Intrusion Detection System (IDS) rules in real-time from Cyber Threat Intelligence (CTI) data.  FALCON uses Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate CTI into deployable Snort (network-based) or YARA (host-based) rules.  A key aspect of FALCON is its multi-phased validation pipeline, which includes syntactic, semantic (using a novel CTI-to-IDS rule semantic similarity scoring model), and performance validation. The framework also determines if an existing rule should be updated or a new rule should be created. The authors demonstrate the system's efficacy with experiments, including evaluations of the semantic scorer, the LLM rule generator, and the overall pipeline, showing high accuracy and inter-rater agreement among security analysts. Finally, the authors constructed a dataset for training and evaluating FALCON which will be made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper tackles an important and timely problem: the rapid evolution of cyber threats demands more agile and scalable methods for generating and maintaining IDS rules.  While autonomous IDS rule generation isn't entirely new, FALCON presents several novel aspects that justify its contribution:

*   **Agentic LLM-driven Pipeline:**  The agentic approach, with its inherent feedback loops and internal validation, distinguishes FALCON from simpler one-shot LLM-based solutions.  The integration of planning, reasoning, and iterative refinement is crucial for producing deployable, high-quality rules. Using LLMs in cybersecurity is not novel, but the agentic system with internal evaluations is a key differentiator.

*   **Novel CTI-to-IDS Semantic Scorer:** This is a significant contribution. Existing code similarity metrics are not well-suited for the unique characteristics of CTI and IDS rules.  The contrastively trained bi-encoder aims to capture the underlying logical consistency between CTI descriptions (often natural language) and the more structured IDS rule syntax. Demonstrating the model captured more than just surface level semantic similiarities is crucial and a strength of the paper. The paper also justified it's usage by performing detailed comparisons againist existing models and demonstrated a novel scoring model was superior for functional aligntment.

*   **Adaptive Rule Management:**  The ability to determine whether to create a new rule or update an existing one is essential for preventing rule bloat and optimizing IDS performance. This feature is less emphasized in the paper but represents a useful practical enhancement.

*   **Comprehensive Evaluation and Dataset:**  The paper goes beyond simply generating rules and provides a rigorous evaluation of the various components. The inter-rater agreement among security analysts is particularly valuable for demonstrating the practical utility of the generated rules. The publically available dataset is also a significant contribution that will allow future researchers to perform further evaluations.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Performance Evaluation Depth:** While the paper mentions performance validation, the details of how this is implemented and measured are somewhat limited.  A more detailed discussion of the performance metrics (e.g., execution time, resource usage) and the results of performance testing would strengthen the paper. It's not completely clear how much the rules created improve on human created rules.
*   **Limited Discussion of Failure Cases:**  While the accuracy is impressive, a discussion of the types of errors FALCON makes (e.g., what types of CTI lead to incorrect rules or rules that fail validation) would provide valuable insights for future improvements.
*   **Lack of Comparison on similar agentic frameworks:** There are existing agentic frameworks that are used in different domains, comparing FALCON's implementation to other frameworks would be beneficial.

**Significance:**

FALCON has the potential to significantly impact the field of cyber threat intelligence and intrusion detection. By automating the rule generation process and incorporating rigorous validation, it can help security teams keep pace with the evolving threat landscape and improve the overall effectiveness of their IDS deployments.  The semantic scorer offers a valuable tool for assessing the quality and relevance of IDS rules, and the adaptive rule management capabilities can help optimize IDS performance.

**Justification for Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7.5**. This score reflects a balance between the paper's strengths and weaknesses. The agentic architecture, the novel semantic scorer, and the comprehensive evaluation are all significant contributions. However, the limited details on performance evaluation and the absence of failure analysis prevent the paper from achieving a higher score. If the performance section were beefed up and a more detailed analysis of the weaknesses of FALCON were included, the paper could be approaching a score of 8 or even 8.5. The public dataset will undoubtedly facilitate further work in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Attention2Probability: Attention-Driven Terminology Probability Estimation for Robust Speech-to-Text System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18701v1)
- **Authors**: Yanfan Du, Jun Zhang, Bin Wang, Jin Qiu, Lu Huang, Yuan Ge, Xiaoqian Liu, Tong Xiao, Jingbo Zhu
- **Abstract**: Recent advances in speech large language models (SLMs) have improved speech recognition and translation in general domains, but accurately generating domain-specific terms or neologisms remains challenging. To address this, we propose Attention2Probability: attention-driven terminology probability estimation for robust speech-to-text system, which is lightweight, flexible, and accurate. Attention2Probability converts cross-attention weights between speech and terminology into presence probabilities, and it further employs curriculum learning to enhance retrieval accuracy. Furthermore, to tackle the lack of data for speech-to-text tasks with terminology intervention, we create and release a new speech dataset with terminology to support future research in this area. Experimental results show that Attention2Probability significantly outperforms the VectorDB method on our test set. Specifically, its maximum recall rates reach 92.57% for Chinese and 86.83% for English. This high recall is achieved with a latency of only 8.71ms per query. Intervening in SLMs' recognition and translation tasks using Attention2Probability-retrieved terms improves terminology accuracy by 6-17%, while revealing that the current utilization of terminology by SLMs has limitations.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Attention2Probability: Attention-Driven Terminology Probability Estimation for Robust Speech-to-Text System" addresses the challenge of accurately generating domain-specific terms in speech-to-text (STT) and speech translation (ST) systems using Speech Large Language Models (SLMs). The authors propose a method called Attention2Probability (A2P) which replaces traditional VectorDB-based retrieval with a cross-attention mechanism. A2P converts cross-attention weights between speech input and terminology into probabilities representing the presence of terms in the speech. It also uses curriculum learning to improve retrieval accuracy.  To address the lack of training data, the authors create and release a new speech dataset with terminology.  Experiments demonstrate that A2P outperforms VectorDB in recall, while improving terminology accuracy in downstream SLM recognition and translation tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in replacing the conventional VectorDB approach with a cross-attention mechanism for terminology retrieval in speech tasks. This is a significant departure from existing retrieval augmented generation (RAG) frameworks in speech-to-text that rely on embeddings and cosine similarity. Converting attention weights directly to probabilities is a novel technique. The curriculum learning approach tailored to term length heterogeneity is also a good contribution. While the individual components (cross-attention, curriculum learning) are not entirely novel in isolation, their combination in this specific application within a speech-to-text context with terminology intervention is unique. The creation and release of a dedicated dataset, even if repurposed, is also a valuable contribution due to the lack of resources in this specific area.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical limitation of SLMs in specialized domains where terminology generation is crucial. The proposed A2P method offers a lightweight and potentially more accurate alternative to VectorDB, reducing reliance on large-scale datasets for training modality alignment models. The empirical results showing improved recall and terminology accuracy are promising, suggesting that A2P can effectively enhance the robustness of STT and ST systems in specialized domains.  The analysis regarding current SLMs struggling to utilize retrieved terminology effectively is an important finding that highlights limitations in the way terms are utilized by SLMs, and opens the door for further exploration in this area.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The proposed method is well-motivated and clearly explained.
    *   The experimental evaluation is thorough, including comparisons to a strong baseline (VectorDB), ablation studies, and analysis of different audio encoders.
    *   The paper identifies and analyzes critical limitations of current SLMs in terminology utilization.
    *   The paper provides a novel dataset for speech to text with terminology which can be used by future research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on Qwen2-Audio-Instruction might limit the generalizability of the findings to other SLM architectures.  The performance dependency on the audio encoder indicates a need for more robust solutions.
    *   The latency analysis reveals a significant bottleneck in the Top-k selection algorithm.  This needs to be addressed to make A2P more practical for real-time applications. Further speed improvements using GPU optimization would be beneficial.
    *   The experiments could benefit from testing on multiple datasets (even if repurposed). Relying primarily on a dataset constructed by the authors, while understandable given the data scarcity, weakens the validation to some extent.
    *   While terminology accuracy improves, some limitations remain within ST tasks. Furthermore, SLM performance doesn't strictly improve with increasing terminology volume, which indicates more investigation is needed.
    *   While the paper explores the typology of the languages it tested in and gives a plausible explanation for its effect, additional explanation could be provided for why the A2P approach does not work on English with a SONAR audio encoder.
    *   It would be useful to do additional tests to demonstrate how well the approach performs when the terminology included in the prompt is only "partially correct," a scenario that is more likely to happen in real-world use-cases.

*   **Potential Impact:** The A2P method has the potential to improve the accuracy and robustness of STT and ST systems in specialized domains, making them more useful for real-world applications. The release of the new dataset can stimulate further research in this area.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-motivated approach to terminology retrieval in speech tasks, addressing a critical limitation of current SLMs. The A2P method achieves promising results, demonstrating improved recall and terminology accuracy compared to a strong baseline. The creation and release of a new dataset is also a significant contribution, due to the data scarcity involved in speech-to-text with terminology.

However, the reliance on a specific SLM architecture, performance dependency on audio encoder, latency issues, and limited dataset variety weaken the overall impact. While the identified limitations in SLM terminology utilization are insightful, further research is needed to address these challenges effectively. A higher score would be warranted if these weaknesses were addressed, and the generalizability and scalability of A2P were demonstrated more convincingly.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Filtering for Creativity: Adaptive Prompting for Multilingual Riddle Generation in LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18709v1)
- **Authors**: Duy Le, Kent Ziti, Evan Girard-Sun, Sean O'Brien, Vasu Sharma, Kevin Zhu
- **Abstract**: Multilingual riddle generation challenges large language models (LLMs) to balance cultural fluency with creative abstraction. Standard prompting strategies -- zero-shot, few-shot, chain-of-thought -- tend to reuse memorized riddles or perform shallow paraphrasing. We introduce Adaptive Originality Filtering (AOF), a prompting framework that filters redundant generations using cosine-based similarity rejection, while enforcing lexical novelty and cross-lingual fidelity. Evaluated across three LLMs and four language pairs, AOF-enhanced GPT-4o achieves \texttt{0.177} Self-BLEU and \texttt{0.915} Distinct-2 in Japanese, signaling improved lexical diversity and reduced redundancy compared to other prompting methods and language pairs. Our findings show that semantic rejection can guide culturally grounded, creative generation without task-specific fine-tuning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Adaptive Originality Filtering (AOF), a prompting framework designed to improve the creativity and cultural relevance of multilingual riddle generation by large language models (LLMs). AOF filters redundant generations using cosine similarity rejection, enforces lexical novelty, and maintains cross-lingual fidelity. The method is evaluated on three LLMs (GPT-4o, LLaMA 3.1, DeepSeek) across four language pairs, comparing against standard prompting baselines using metrics for lexical diversity, redundancy, bilingual semantic alignment, and syntactic validity.  The results demonstrate that AOF enhances both originality and cross-cultural alignment, indicating a lightweight approach to guiding LLMs towards more creative and culturally attuned multilingual generation without requiring task-specific fine-tuning.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of AOF, using semantic filtering within a prompting framework to enhance creativity and reduce redundancy in multilingual riddle generation, possesses a degree of novelty. Prior work has focused more on riddle *solving* rather than *generation*, and the explicit focus on culturally grounded and lexically novel generation *via prompting alone* differentiates this work from those employing fine-tuning or complex decoding strategies. However, rejection sampling and similarity-based filtering are not entirely new concepts in NLP, so the novelty lies primarily in their specific application to the multilingual creative generation task. The novelty is mainly incremental as the techniques used (rejection sampling, cosine similarity) are well-established but are combined in a novel way and applied to a new task.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a key challenge with LLMs: their tendency to reproduce memorized content or perform shallow paraphrasing, especially in creative tasks across multiple languages. By demonstrating that careful prompting, incorporating external filtering, can significantly improve the originality and cultural relevance of generated riddles, the paper offers a practical and scalable approach for guiding LLMs toward more nuanced and culturally aware outputs. The finding that semantic rejection can guide culturally grounded, creative generation without task-specific fine-tuning is valuable, suggesting a potential pathway for enhancing LLM performance in other creative domains. The contribution is particularly significant because it focuses on a low-resource and easily implementable prompting technique rather than relying on computationally expensive fine-tuning, which often introduces other biases.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clearly Defined Problem and Solution:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of standard prompting techniques in creative multilingual generation and proposes a well-defined AOF framework to address these limitations.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation is conducted across multiple models, languages, and prompting baselines, providing a robust assessment of AOF's effectiveness.  The use of various metrics (Self-BLEU, Distinct-n, BERTScore) provides a comprehensive picture of the method's impact on originality, diversity, and alignment.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The inclusion of qualitative examples and discussion adds depth to the quantitative results, providing insights into the nuances of AOF's effects on metaphor, creativity, and syntactic variation.
    *   **Lightweight and Scalable Approach:** AOF provides a method for improving LLM outputs with a simple, prompting based approach which is easily scalable and does not require extensive fine-tuning.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Dataset Scope:** The study focuses on the BiRdQA dataset, which may limit the generalizability of the findings to broader creative tasks.
    *   **Surface-Level Novelty:** The MiniLM-based filtering emphasizes surface-level novelty, potentially missing deeper semantic redundancy.
    *   **Human Evaluations:** The paper acknowledges that a more in-depth human evaluation (e.g., creativity rating, solving rates) would further strengthen the study. The full human evaluations were not fully documented.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** Rejection sampling and cosine similarity are not novel methods. While their application here is original, the building blocks themselves are well-established.
    *   **Lack of Error Analysis:** The paper lacks a detailed error analysis of why AOF fails. While the paper demonstrates improvements, it's unclear in what scenarios AOF would not perform as expected.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper's findings could influence prompt design practices for creative multilingual generation tasks.  The AOF framework offers a template for incorporating external filtering into prompting strategies, potentially inspiring further research on methods for guiding LLMs toward more original and culturally aware outputs. The techniques could also be extended to other generation tasks beyond riddles, potentially influencing broader NLG practices.

**Justification for Score:**

AOF provides a practical and scalable method for enhancing the creativity and cultural relevance of LLM outputs for multilingual riddle generation. Given that riddle generation falls on a niche area within NLG, the study's limited human evaluation and limited novelty mean that the score is below what is typically expected for a more general contribution.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### EMMM, Explain Me My Model! Explainable Machine Generated Text Detection in Dialogues
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18715v1)
- **Authors**: Angela Yifei Yuan, Haoyi Li, Soyeon Caren Han, Christopher Leckie
- **Abstract**: The rapid adoption of large language models (LLMs) in customer service introduces new risks, as malicious actors can exploit them to conduct large-scale user impersonation through machine-generated text (MGT). Current MGT detection methods often struggle in online conversational settings, reducing the reliability and interpretability essential for trustworthy AI deployment. In customer service scenarios where operators are typically non-expert users, explanation become crucial for trustworthy MGT detection. In this paper, we propose EMMM, an explanation-then-detection framework that balances latency, accuracy, and non-expert-oriented interpretability. Experimental results demonstrate that EMMM provides explanations accessible to non-expert users, with 70\% of human evaluators preferring its outputs, while achieving competitive accuracy compared to state-of-the-art models and maintaining low latency, generating outputs within 1 second. Our code and dataset are open-sourced at https://github.com/AngieYYF/EMMM-explainable-chatbot-detection.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can help with that. Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "EMMM, Explain Me My Model! Explainable Machine Generated Text Detection in Dialogues":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EMMM, an "explanation-then-detection" framework designed to detect machine-generated text (MGT) in online conversational settings. It addresses the challenges of asymmetric detection in dialogues, the need for user-friendly explanations for non-expert users, and the difficulty of interpreting local attribution explanations in MGT detection models. EMMM integrates multi-dimensional inputs (behaviors and language), operates at multi-levels (turn and dialogue), and employs multi-strategy explanations (local natural language explanations, and semi-global visual insights).  It leverages dialogue act theory to enhance interpretability and provides explanations accessible to non-expert users in customer service scenarios.  The authors demonstrate that EMMM provides explanations preferred by human evaluators while maintaining competitive accuracy and low latency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution is the EMMM framework itself, which appears to be a novel approach to explainable MGT detection specifically tailored for conversational settings. Integrating multi-dimensional inputs (language + dialogue acts), operating at multiple levels of interaction, and offering multi-strategy explanations is a significant advance over existing MGT detection methods, which tend to focus on isolated text passages and lack comprehensive explainability features. The focus on non-expert user interpretability is also novel.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the increasing prevalence of LLMs in customer service and the associated risks of malicious actors impersonating users.  Reliable and *trustworthy* MGT detection is crucial.  By addressing the interpretability challenges, EMMM aims to increase user trust and facilitate better moderation decisions. Its effectiveness in real-world deployment, achieving real-time explainability, makes it valuable for AI safety.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing MGT detection methods in conversational contexts and the importance of explainability.

    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** The EMMM framework is well-structured and integrates various components logically. The sequential selector-predictor design optimizes both performance and interpretability.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper includes a thorough evaluation with multiple datasets, relevant baselines, and both quantitative (detection performance, explanation relevance, time complexity) and qualitative (human survey) metrics.
    *   **Focus on User Needs:**  The human-centered design of explanations, incorporating HCI principles and feedback from non-expert users, is a key strength.

    *   **Demonstrated Effectiveness:** The experimental results show that EMMM achieves competitive detection accuracy while providing more user-friendly explanations than existing methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** While the focus on customer service dialogues is practical, it also limits the generalizability of the framework. Its performance and applicability in other conversational domains (e.g., social media, online forums) need further investigation.

    *   **Dependency on Dialogue Act Extraction:**  EMMM relies on accurate dialogue act extraction, and the performance is likely sensitive to the quality of the DA extraction model. The use of an LLM for DA extraction on the Frames dataset, although a sensible few-shot approach, can be a potential performance bottleneck as shown in table 3.
    *   **Limited Interpretability Evaluation:** While the human survey provides valuable insights, a more in-depth qualitative analysis of user understanding and trust in the explanations would be beneficial. The number of participants (13) could be considered relatively small.

    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While it integrates several techniques, the individual techniques such as the sequential selector-predictor pipeline itself may not be fully original, and it’s the combination of these ideas into a cohesive framework that provides the core value, reducing the individual contribution's impact.
    *   **Dependency on Parameter Tuning:** EMMM achieves good performance after manually finding the best parameters. Performance is tightly linked to good parameter tuning.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of MGT detection by promoting more human-centered and transparent approaches.  It could inspire further research on explainable AI in conversational settings and the development of more practical and trustworthy MGT detection systems. It also demonstrates the value of integrating dialogue act theory and HCI principles into MGT detection.

**Justification:**

EMMM advances beyond standard MGT detection by its focus on explainability, real-time performance, and suitability for use by non-expert users. While the framework relies on and integrates existing techniques, the way they are combined and specifically tailored for conversational contexts makes this work both novel and influential for human-aligned AI safety solutions. However, the dependence on DA extraction, the limited generalizability, and the reliance on parameter tuning should be carefully noted.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### VistaWise: Building Cost-Effective Agent with Cross-Modal Knowledge Graph for Minecraft
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18722v1)
- **Authors**: Honghao Fu, Junlong Ren, Qi Chai, Deheng Ye, Yujun Cai, Hao Wang
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown significant promise in embodied decision-making tasks within virtual open-world environments. Nonetheless, their performance is hindered by the absence of domain-specific knowledge. Methods that finetune on large-scale domain-specific data entail prohibitive development costs. This paper introduces VistaWise, a cost-effective agent framework that integrates cross-modal domain knowledge and finetunes a dedicated object detection model for visual analysis. It reduces the requirement for domain-specific training data from millions of samples to a few hundred. VistaWise integrates visual information and textual dependencies into a cross-modal knowledge graph (KG), enabling a comprehensive and accurate understanding of multimodal environments. We also equip the agent with a retrieval-based pooling strategy to extract task-related information from the KG, and a desktop-level skill library to support direct operation of the Minecraft desktop client via mouse and keyboard inputs. Experimental results demonstrate that VistaWise achieves state-of-the-art performance across various open-world tasks, highlighting its effectiveness in reducing development costs while enhancing agent performance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the VistaWise paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces VistaWise, a framework for building cost-effective agents for the Minecraft environment.  It addresses the limitations of current LLM-based agents, which often rely on environment APIs or require extensive domain-specific finetuning.  VistaWise integrates a finetuned object detection model for visual perception with a cross-modal knowledge graph (KG). This KG combines visual information and external textual knowledge to provide a comprehensive understanding of the environment. The agent uses a retrieval-based graph-pooling strategy to extract task-relevant information from the KG, along with a desktop-level skill library (using PyAutoGUI) to directly control the Minecraft client through mouse and keyboard inputs.  The experimental results claim state-of-the-art performance in various open-world tasks, particularly in "obtaining diamond," while significantly reducing data collection and training costs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper has several novel aspects:

*   **Cost-effective Knowledge Integration:**  The most significant contribution is the architecture that allows LLMs to perform embodied tasks in a virtual world such as Minecraft without relying on external APIs, and by only finetuning a object detection module with very little data. This contrasts with many approaches which use LLM vision modules or involve finetuning LLMs with orders of magnitude more domain-specific data.
*   **Cross-Modal Knowledge Graph:** The KG structure combining visual information from a vision model with textual knowledge is an approach that is novel in the Minecraft environment.
*   **Retrieval-Based Graph Pooling:**  The strategy of retrieving task-related information from the KG using prompt-guided graph traversal (PSP and EMP) is a novel approach to knowledge retrieval in embodied environments.
*   **Desktop-Level Skill Library:** Using PyAutoGUI to control the game directly at the desktop level avoids API dependency. However, the action space is still relatively simple, and there's nothing incredibly novel about leveraging existing libraries for this.

**Significance:**

*   **Reduced Data and Training Cost:** The demonstrated reduction in data requirements and GPU resources is impactful. It makes training agents for complex virtual environments more accessible and practical.  This is especially important for scaling up such agents.
*   **Improved Generalization:** By avoiding direct API dependency, the framework theoretically allows better generalization to different environments and Minecraft versions. However, this generalization is not explicitly demonstrated in the paper.
*   **State-of-the-art Performance:** The claim of achieving state-of-the-art performance on the "obtain diamond" task is significant, but it's crucial to scrutinize the experimental setup and compare it directly against existing baselines in a carefully controlled manner.
* **Reproducibility:** They've also included the code, model and dataset which is also a major plus.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Experimental Rigor:** While the paper presents results, the experimental details can be stronger. A clearer explanation of how the baseline methods were implemented and tuned in the same environment is important. It is also not clear what version and parameters of Minecraft environment that the experiments were conducted.
*   **Generalization Claims:** The paper claims that the model does not need an API to function but does not compare it to an API based model. The current version of the paper does not have sufficient evidence to support the claim that the design is more generalizable.
*   **Action Space:** The action space is fairly limited by the skill library with discrete actions, and does not have sufficient detail on the actions that are available to the agent.
*   **Limited Ablation Studies**: The studies in the paper are not detailed enough, it does not explain why the authors choose the metrics or parameters for the study.
*   **Desktop-Level Control Limited by Screen Size and Obstacles:** The desktop level control in Minecraft is only as effective as the player's ability to see obstacles.

**Potential Influence:**

The paper can potentially influence the field by providing a pathway for building LLM-based agents that:

*   Require less domain-specific data.
*   Avoid dependency on environment APIs.
*   Are more cost-effective to train and deploy.

This could lead to wider adoption of LLM agents in virtual environments and enable research in more complex and scalable scenarios.

**Justification for Score:**

VistaWise presents a solid engineering approach that addresses a key challenge in LLM-based embodied agents: the high cost of knowledge acquisition. The novelty lies in the architecture, the cross-modal KG, retrieval-based pooling, and the integration of desktop-level control. The claimed state-of-the-art performance and reduced data requirements are significant.

However, the experimental rigor needs strengthening. The paper needs to better demonstrate the advantages over API-based models and needs more information on the action space and game. The performance numbers are also not significantly better.

Therefore, based on these aspects, I assign the following score:

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Bias Mitigation Agent: Optimizing Source Selection for Fair and Balanced Knowledge Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18724v1)
- **Authors**: Karanbir Singh, Deepak Muppiri, William Ngu
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have transformed the field of artificial intelligence by unlocking the era of generative applications. Built on top of generative AI capabilities, Agentic AI represents a major shift toward autonomous, goal-driven systems that can reason, retrieve, and act. However, they also inherit the bias present in both internal and external information sources. This significantly affects the fairness and balance of retrieved information, and hence reduces user trust. To address this critical challenge, we introduce a novel Bias Mitigation Agent, a multi-agent system designed to orchestrate the workflow of bias mitigation through specialized agents that optimize the selection of sources to ensure that the retrieved content is both highly relevant and minimally biased to promote fair and balanced knowledge dissemination. The experimental results demonstrate an 81.82\% reduction in bias compared to a baseline naive retrieval strategy.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary of the paper followed by a critical evaluation, and finally, the score.

**Paper Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel "Bias Mitigation Agent," a multi-agent system designed to reduce bias in knowledge retrieval within agentic AI workflows. The core idea is to optimize the selection of information sources before ingestion by the agent, thereby promoting fairer and more balanced knowledge dissemination. The agent incorporates a Manager Agent that coordinates Worker Agents, each specialized for tasks like knowledge retrieval, bias detection (using Dbias), and source selection. The source selection process can operate in three modes: "No Source Selection" (baseline), "Zero-Shot" (rule-based selection based on relevance and bias scores), and "Few-Shot" (selection guided by in-context learning). The paper evaluates the framework using news articles from MBIC and BABE datasets, using OpenAI's GPT models as reasoning engines. The results demonstrate that the Zero-Shot approach significantly reduces bias compared to the baseline, although at the cost of increased latency. The Few-Shot approach provides a balance between bias reduction and latency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses an Important Problem:** The paper tackles a crucial challenge in the burgeoning field of agentic AI: bias in knowledge retrieval. As agents become more autonomous and reliant on external data, mitigating bias is paramount for ensuring fairness, trustworthiness, and user acceptance.
    *   **Novel Architecture:** The multi-agent architecture is a sound approach to decompose the complex task of bias mitigation into manageable components. The use of a Manager Agent to oversee the process provides a structured and controllable workflow. The use of LangGraph is also a strength.
    *   **Clear Operational Modes:** The three operational modes (No Source Selection, Zero-Shot, Few-Shot) offer a flexible spectrum of trade-offs between bias reduction, efficiency, and complexity. This allows users to tailor the system to their specific needs and constraints.
    *   **Quantitative Evaluation:** The paper provides a quantitative evaluation of the framework's performance, using well-defined metrics for relevance, bias confidence, latency, and retry rates. The comparison against a baseline ("No Source Selection") provides a clear benchmark for assessing the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The use of well-known datasets MBIC and BABE is also a strength.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The framework seems reasonably reproducible. The paper details the architecture, the algorithms used for bias detection (Dbias), and the experimental setup. While code availability isn't explicitly mentioned, the descriptions are sufficiently detailed to allow for implementation.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Existing Bias Detection:** The "Bias Detection Agent" relies on the Dbias pipeline, which is itself a pre-existing tool. The novelty is primarily in the architecture and orchestration of agents, rather than in a groundbreaking bias detection technique. Also, the Dbias tool, while helpful, may not capture all nuanced forms of bias. The quality of the Bias Mitigation Agent depends on the quality of the chosen Dbias tool.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Bias Types:** The paper primarily focuses on bias as detected by the Dbias pipeline, which appears to focus on textual bias in news articles. It does not explicitly address other forms of bias that can arise in knowledge retrieval, such as selection bias or confirmation bias or those arising from specific knowledge domains.
    *   **Generalizability Concerns:** The experiments are conducted using news articles from two specific datasets. The results may not generalize to other domains or types of information sources. For instance, the effectiveness of the framework may vary in technical domains or when dealing with highly subjective content.
    *   **Scalability:** The multi-agent architecture, while conceptually sound, may introduce scalability challenges, particularly in scenarios involving large-scale knowledge retrieval and complex user queries. The overhead of coordinating multiple agents and performing bias detection on numerous candidate documents could become computationally expensive.
    *   **Lack of Ablation Studies:** It would be beneficial to include ablation studies to understand the contribution of each individual agent to the overall performance of the system. This would help isolate the impact of each component and identify potential bottlenecks.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis:** The paper focuses mainly on quantitative metrics. While these are important, incorporating a qualitative analysis of the generated responses and the selected sources would provide richer insights into the framework's behavior and limitations.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's novelty lies primarily in its architectural design and the integration of a multi-agent system for bias mitigation in agentic knowledge retrieval. The combination of specialized agents, the supervisor agent, and the different operational modes represents a unique approach to this problem.
    *   The significance of the work is that it provides a concrete and potentially practical framework for addressing a growing concern in the field of agentic AI. The quantitative results demonstrate the potential for bias reduction, even if further research is needed to address some of the limitations.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    *   The paper could stimulate further research in the area of bias-aware agent design and workflow optimization.
    *   The modular architecture and the clear definition of agent roles could serve as a blueprint for other researchers developing similar systems.
    *   The empirical evaluation could provide a benchmark for comparing different bias mitigation approaches in knowledge retrieval.

**Score:** 6

**Rationale:**

The paper addresses a relevant problem with a well-structured multi-agent approach that delivers a measurable improvement over a baseline. However, the reliance on an existing bias detection technique limits its novelty. Furthermore, the scope of the evaluation is confined to news articles and the lack of ablation studies and a qualitative analysis weakens the validation. The scalability concerns are also not addressed. The architectural design of the system is well-conceived, and the empirical results show a promising reduction in bias, although with trade-offs in latency.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 6/10

### Beyond Tokens: Enhancing RTL Quality Estimation via Structural Graph Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18730v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Liu, Hongji Zhang, Yiwen Wang, Dimitris Tsaras, Lei Chen, Mingxuan Yuan, Qiang Xu
- **Abstract**: Estimating the quality of register transfer level (RTL) designs is crucial in the electronic design automation (EDA) workflow, as it enables instant feedback on key metrics like area and delay without the need for time-consuming logic synthesis. While recent approaches have leveraged large language models (LLMs) to derive embeddings from RTL code and achieved promising results, they overlook the structural semantics essential for accurate quality estimation. In contrast, the control data flow graph (CDFG) view exposes the design's structural characteristics more explicitly, offering richer cues for representation learning. In this work, we introduce a novel structure-aware graph self-supervised learning framework, StructRTL, for improved RTL design quality estimation. By learning structure-informed representations from CDFGs, our method significantly outperforms prior art on various quality estimation tasks. To further boost performance, we incorporate a knowledge distillation strategy that transfers low-level insights from post-mapping netlists into the CDFG predictor. Experiments show that our approach establishes new state-of-the-art results, demonstrating the effectiveness of combining structural learning with cross-stage supervision.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Beyond Tokens: Enhancing RTL Quality Estimation via Structural Graph Learning" introduces StructRTL, a novel framework for estimating the quality of Register Transfer Level (RTL) hardware designs.  Instead of using token-based views like previous LLM-based approaches, StructRTL operates on the Control Data Flow Graph (CDFG) representation of the RTL, which explicitly captures the design's structural semantics. The framework employs two self-supervised pretraining tasks: structure-aware masked node modeling and edge prediction, to learn structure-informed representations from CDFGs. Furthermore, the authors incorporate a knowledge distillation strategy, transferring low-level insights from post-mapping netlists into the CDFG predictor.  Experiments demonstrate that StructRTL outperforms existing methods on various quality estimation tasks, achieving state-of-the-art results.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:*

The paper demonstrates a reasonable level of novelty.

1.  **CDFG-based approach:** Moving away from token-based LLM approaches and using CDFG to represent RTL designs is a step toward capturing structural information and semantics more explicitly. While CDFGs have been used previously, their application in conjunction with self-supervised learning and knowledge distillation within an RTL quality estimation framework is a novel contribution.
2.  **Self-supervised pretraining tasks tailored for CDFGs:** Designing structure-aware masked node modeling and edge prediction tasks specifically adapted to CDFGs is a key contribution. These tasks address the limitations of applying generic graph self-supervised learning methods directly to computational graphs like CDFGs.
3.  **Knowledge Distillation**: Using post-mapping netlist for knowledge distillation into RTL models is a reasonable method for cross-stage information transfer.

*Significance:*

The significance of the paper lies in its potential to improve the efficiency of hardware design. Faster and more accurate RTL quality estimation can significantly reduce design turnaround time and improve design productivity. The paper's results show a clear improvement over existing methods, suggesting that StructRTL could have a practical impact.

**Strengths:**

*   **Strong empirical results:** The paper presents convincing experimental results demonstrating StructRTL's superior performance compared to both graph-based and LLM-based baselines. The ablation study further supports the effectiveness of the proposed pretraining tasks.
*   **Well-defined methodology:** The paper provides a clear and detailed description of the StructRTL framework, including the CDFG construction process, model architecture, and pretraining tasks.
*   **Addresses a practical problem:** RTL quality estimation is a crucial task in hardware design, and the paper proposes a solution that can potentially improve design efficiency.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **CDFG Construction Complexity**: The paper uses commercial RTL to RTLIL tools for CDFG construction. More work can be done on demonstrating open-source CDFG generation.
*   **Limited Generalization**:  The dataset is a set of designs, likely representative of many RTL designs. Although large, it does not exhaustively cover all RTL design styles or complexities. It is important to note that datasets used by the authors are filtered to remove designs that could not be converted into CDFGs or synthesized, limiting generalizability.
*   **Scope of Validation Metrics:** The validation uses area and delay, the standard metrics in hardware optimization. However, RTL also involves power and testability concerns. Further investigation would strengthen the real-world impact.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity**: The impact of hyperparameter choices (e.g., number of GNN layers, balancing coefficients) could be further investigated to ensure the robustness and reliability of the proposed method.

**Score Justification:**

I assign a score of 7 to this paper. The paper demonstrates a solid contribution with a clearly defined method and thorough experimental evaluation. The integration of CDFGs, self-supervised learning tasks tailored for CDFGs, and cross-stage knowledge distillation is well executed and leads to state-of-the-art results. While there are limitations, particularly in the scope of the design validation metrics and further investigation into other concerns with CDFG generation, the paper is a meaningful advancement in RTL quality estimation. It opens promising avenues for future research in structure-aware learning for hardware design.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking Caching for LLM Serving Systems: Beyond Traditional Heuristics
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18736v1)
- **Authors**: Jungwoo Kim, Minsang Kim, Jaeheon Lee, Chanwoo Moon, Heejin Kim, Taeho Hwang, Woosuk Chung, Yeseong Kim, Sungjin Lee
- **Abstract**: Serving Large Language Models (LLMs) at scale requires meeting strict Service Level Objectives (SLOs) under severe computational and memory constraints. Nevertheless, traditional caching strategies fall short: exact-matching and prefix caches neglect query semantics, while state-of-the-art semantic caches remain confined to traditional intuitions, offering little conceptual departure. Building on this, we present SISO, a semantic caching system that redefines efficiency for LLM serving. SISO introduces centroid-based caching to maximize coverage with minimal memory, locality-aware replacement to preserve high-value entries, and dynamic thresholding to balance accuracy and latency under varying workloads. Across diverse datasets, SISO delivers up to 1.71$\times$ higher hit ratios and consistently stronger SLO attainment compared to state-of-the-art systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Rethinking Caching for LLM Serving Systems: Beyond Traditional Heuristics":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SISO, a novel semantic caching system designed to improve the efficiency of serving Large Language Models (LLMs).  SISO departs from traditional caching heuristics by incorporating three key features: centroid-based caching (grouping similar queries under a single centroid), semantic locality-aware centroid replacement (prioritizing centroids representing frequently accessed semantic regions), and dynamic threshold adjustment (adapting the similarity threshold based on workload intensity to balance accuracy and latency).  The authors demonstrate through experiments with real-world datasets and LLMs that SISO achieves higher hit ratios and consistently stronger Service Level Objective (SLO) attainment compared to state-of-the-art semantic caching systems. The system adaptively adjusts caching behaviour in response to changing system load, trading off output quality for faster results if required.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper demonstrates moderate novelty. While semantic caching itself isn't new (GPTCache is a key reference), the combination of centroid-based caching, semantic locality-aware replacement, and dynamic threshold adjustment presents a new approach to caching LLM queries. The core innovations are the _orchestration_ of these techniques in a way that is demonstrably effective. Centroid-based caching, although inspired by clustering techniques, hasn't been thoroughly explored for LLM serving. The exploitation of semantic locality as a metric for replacement is also a valuable addition. Dynamic thresholding, although conceptually straightforward, is effectively implemented in SISO as it adapts to instantaneous requirements of the system.

*   **Significance:** The work has significant practical implications. LLM serving is computationally expensive, and any technique that reduces the load on the LLM inference engine is highly valuable. The experimental results clearly show that SISO outperforms existing methods in terms of hit ratio and SLO attainment.  The benefits are especially pronounced under high load or variable workload conditions, which are common in real-world LLM deployments. The trade-off between accuracy and latency, while present in other caching systems, is addressed in a more fine-grained and dynamic manner within SISO, making it practically more useful. Importantly, the approach avoids intrusive system-wide modifications, making it more portable and compatible with existing infrastructures.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem statement and clear motivation.
    *   Sound design principles based on comprehensive analysis of LLM queries.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation using diverse datasets and LLM models.
    *   Comparison against relevant state-of-the-art systems.
    *   Adaptation based on queuing theory to maintain service-level objectives.
    *   The techniques are implemented in a practical way, suitable for adoption into existing LLM infrastructure.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limitations: The paper acknowledges limitations related to multi-turn queries and tasks where minor input changes dramatically alter the output (e.g., coding and debugging).  The system shows limited benefit for brainstorm query styles, which are acknowledged in the discussion. A more in-depth discussion about how to extend the proposed approach to address these cases would strengthen the work.
    *   Accuracy drop: A marginal accuracy drop of 6.9% is observed. While considered marginal by the authors, more detailed analyses relating to edge cases would be helpful.
    *   Complexity: Although presented as portable, the implementation requires query log analysis and an understanding of clustering methods. This adds a degree of complexity to deployment relative to simpler caching techniques. The reliance on HNSW also necessitates a vector database and requires specific consideration from system architects to configure for optimal caching performance.

*   **Potential Influence:** SISO has the potential to influence the design of future LLM serving systems by promoting a more semantic-aware approach to caching. The ideas of centroid-based caching and semantic locality-aware replacement are likely to be adopted and extended in other systems.  The demonstration of improved SLO attainment is compelling and addresses a key challenge in deploying LLMs at scale.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper delivers a useful, well-engineered, and demonstrably effective LLM caching system. While not revolutionary, the novel combination of techniques and the clear empirical benefits justify a strong score. The work has practical relevance and provides insights that can be used to improve the performance and efficiency of LLM serving systems. The identified limitations provide clear avenues for future research.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.DB
- **Score**: 7/10

### Beyond Quality: Unlocking Diversity in Ad Headline Generation with Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18739v1)
- **Authors**: Chang Wang, Siyu Yan, Depeng Yuan, Yuqi Chen, Yanhua Huang, Yuanhang Zheng, Shuhao Li, Yinqi Zhang, Kedi Chen, Mingrui Zhu, Ruiwen Xu
- **Abstract**: The generation of ad headlines plays a vital role in modern advertising, where both quality and diversity are essential to engage a broad range of audience segments. Current approaches primarily optimize language models for headline quality or click-through rates (CTR), often overlooking the need for diversity and resulting in homogeneous outputs. To address this limitation, we propose DIVER, a novel framework based on large language models (LLMs) that are jointly optimized for both diversity and quality. We first design a semantic- and stylistic-aware data generation pipeline that automatically produces high-quality training pairs with ad content and multiple diverse headlines. To achieve the goal of generating high-quality and diversified ad headlines within a single forward pass, we propose a multi-stage multi-objective optimization framework with supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and reinforcement learning (RL). Experiments on real-world industrial datasets demonstrate that DIVER effectively balances quality and diversity. Deployed on a large-scale content-sharing platform serving hundreds of millions of users, our framework improves advertiser value (ADVV) and CTR by 4.0% and 1.4%.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of generating diverse and high-quality ad headlines using Large Language Models (LLMs). The authors argue that existing approaches primarily optimize for quality and CTR, neglecting the importance of diversity. To overcome this, they propose DIVER, a multi-stage, multi-objective framework. DIVER first uses a semantic- and stylistic-aware data generation pipeline to create a synthetic dataset of ad content paired with multiple diverse headlines. This synthetic data is then used for cold-start supervised fine-tuning (SFT) of an LLM. Finally, multi-objective reinforcement learning (RL) is applied to optimize for both quality and diversity. Experiments on a real-world industrial dataset from Xiaohongshu demonstrate that DIVER effectively balances quality and diversity, leading to improvements in advertiser value (ADVV) and CTR. The framework is deployed on Xiaohongshu, a large-scale content-sharing platform, resulting in improved user engagement and advertiser satisfaction.

**Novelty and Significance Evaluation:**

The paper presents a reasonably novel approach to ad headline generation by focusing on the often overlooked aspect of diversity alongside quality. The key novelty lies in the combination of several techniques into a cohesive framework:

*   **Synthetic Data Generation:** Automatically generating a diverse, high-quality training dataset is a valuable contribution, addressing the difficulty and cost of manual creation. The semantic- and stylistic-aware approach appears well-designed.
