### Enhancing compact convolutional transformers with super attention
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18960v1)
- **Authors**: Simpenzwe Honore Leandre, Natenaile Asmamaw Shiferaw, Dillip Rout
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we propose a vision model that adopts token mixing, sequence-pooling, and convolutional tokenizers to achieve state-of-the-art performance and efficient inference in fixed context-length tasks. In the CIFAR100 benchmark, our model significantly improves the baseline of the top 1% and top 5% validation accuracy from 36.50% to 46.29% and 66.33% to 76.31%, while being more efficient than the Scaled Dot Product Attention (SDPA) transformers when the context length is less than the embedding dimension and only 60% the size. In addition, the architecture demonstrates high training stability and does not rely on techniques such as data augmentation like mixup, positional embeddings, or learning rate scheduling. We make our code available on Github.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a modified compact convolutional transformer architecture that incorporates "super attention" to improve data efficiency and performance on fixed context-length tasks. The approach combines token mixing, sequence pooling, and convolutional tokenizers. The authors test their model on the CIFAR100 dataset, reporting significant improvements in top-1% and top-5% validation accuracy compared to a standard Scaled Dot Product Attention (SDPA) baseline while also reducing the parameter count. They claim their architecture exhibits high training stability and doesn't rely on data augmentation techniques. The code is made available on GitHub.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the specific combination of techniques (super attention + token mixing + sequence pooling) within the compact convolutional transformer framework. While each of these components isn't inherently novel on its own, their integration and demonstrated improvement within this particular architecture contributes to novelty. The claimed reduction in parameter count compared to standard attention mechanisms is also a notable aspect. However, the 'super attention' mechanism is not well described in detail making it hard to discern the truly new aspects to this.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance hinges on its ability to achieve better performance and efficiency compared to existing compact transformers. The CIFAR100 results presented demonstrate a compelling improvement over the SDPA baseline. The claim that the architecture exhibits high training stability and avoids the need for data augmentation adds to its practical value. Reducing the parameter count while increasing accuracy is a desirable property, suggesting that this approach could be more suitable for resource-constrained environments. However, the claims of not needing data augmentation or learning rate scheduling should be backed up by ablation studies to justify the advantages.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear presentation of the architecture.
    *   Demonstrated improvement on a standard benchmark (CIFAR100).
    *   Focus on improving both performance and efficiency (parameter reduction).
    *   Claims of high training stability.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited dataset validation. It should be tested on other datasets beyond CIFAR100.
    *   Lack of in-depth ablation studies. It isn't explicitly clear which component of the architecture contributes most to the performance gain. Is it the super attention mechanism, the token mixing, sequence pooling, or all three working in concert?
    *   The definition and implementation details of "super attention" are vague. More detailed description of its mechanics, including how it differs from standard attention is needed.
    *   The paper does not evaluate against other state-of-the-art transformer architectures or explain clearly the benefits from previous works like Flash Attention.
    *   The comparison to ViT-12/16 is a bit indirect, relying on previous results, and should be benchmarked directly on the models.

*   **Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution by combining existing techniques in a way that leads to improved performance and efficiency in compact convolutional transformers. The CIFAR100 results are promising. However, the lack of thorough ablation studies and clear specification of the "super attention" mechanism, limits the overall impact and scope of the contribution. The novelty exists, but the limited validation holds it back. In addition, the comparison to past works seems a bit weak.

### The Double-edged Sword of LLM-based Data Reconstruction: Understanding and Mitigating Contextual Vulnerability in Word-level Differential Privacy Text Sanitization
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18976v1)
- **Authors**: Stephen Meisenbacher, Alexandra Klymenko, Andreea-Elena Bodea, Florian Matthes
- **Abstract**: Differentially private text sanitization refers to the process of privatizing texts under the framework of Differential Privacy (DP), providing provable privacy guarantees while also empirically defending against adversaries seeking to harm privacy. Despite their simplicity, DP text sanitization methods operating at the word level exhibit a number of shortcomings, among them the tendency to leave contextual clues from the original texts due to randomization during sanitization $\unicode{x2013}$ this we refer to as $\textit{contextual vulnerability}$. Given the powerful contextual understanding and inference capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs), we explore to what extent LLMs can be leveraged to exploit the contextual vulnerability of DP-sanitized texts. We expand on previous work not only in the use of advanced LLMs, but also in testing a broader range of sanitization mechanisms at various privacy levels. Our experiments uncover a double-edged sword effect of LLM-based data reconstruction attacks on privacy and utility: while LLMs can indeed infer original semantics and sometimes degrade empirical privacy protections, they can also be used for good, to improve the quality and privacy of DP-sanitized texts. Based on our findings, we propose recommendations for using LLM data reconstruction as a post-processing step, serving to increase privacy protection by thinking adversarially.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the "contextual vulnerability" of word-level differentially private (DP) text sanitization methods.  It argues that despite providing per-word privacy guarantees, these methods can leak contextual information that, when combined with the inference capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs), can be exploited to reconstruct the original text. The authors extend previous work by using more advanced LLMs in attacks, testing a broader range of sanitization mechanisms at various privacy levels, and ensuring uniform document-level privacy budgets. They find that LLM-based reconstruction can both degrade privacy and improve utility (text coherence), creating a double-edged sword. They propose using LLMs for post-processing to improve privacy and coherence and offer recommendations for effective word-level DP text sanitization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper builds upon existing research about vulnerabilities in word-level DP text sanitization but significantly expands it. While the idea of using LLMs in attacks is not entirely novel, this work is distinguished by
    *   Considering more DP methods (not just SANTEXT)
    *   Rigorous attention to document-level privacy budgets, ensuring a fair evaluation.
    *   Detailed evaluation metrics encompassing semantic similarity, task performance, and text coherence.
    *   Investigating whether LLM reconstruction can actually improve privacy under specific conditions.
*   **Significance:** The paper makes several significant contributions:

    *   **Understanding of Contextual Vulnerability:**  It provides a more comprehensive analysis of how LLMs can exploit vulnerabilities in DP-sanitized text.
    *   **Double-Edged Sword Argument:** It highlights that LLM reconstruction is not just a threat but can also be a tool to improve utility and (potentially) privacy.
    *   **Practical Recommendations:** It offers actionable recommendations for researchers and practitioners using word-level DP, including using LLMs as a post-processing step.
    *   **Reproducibility**: The authors clearly describe their experimental setup, making the work more reproducible. They also release or utilize publicly available code when possible.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-written and clearly organized.
    *   Thorough experimental methodology.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation metrics.
    *   Balanced perspective, acknowledging both the risks and benefits of LLM-based reconstruction.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope:** The work focuses primarily on word-level DP and doesn't address other DP techniques for text. Generalizability is limited.
    *   **Data Contamination Concern:**  The authors acknowledge the possibility of data contamination, which is crucial but difficult to fully address.
    *   **LLM Selection Justification**: While several LLMs were tested, further justification of the specific LLM choices and hyperparameters is desired.

*   **Impact:** This paper is likely to influence future research on DP text sanitization by shifting the focus from simply avoiding LLM-based attacks to actively leveraging LLMs for privacy enhancement. The practical recommendations are valuable for practitioners deploying word-level DP.

**Rigorous Rationale for the Score:**

I am assigning a score of 7.  While the *core idea* of leveraging LLMs to identify privacy leaks in sanitized text isn't entirely new, the systematic and rigorous way this paper explores the trade-offs is. The inclusion of document-level budget constraints was a *key step* that improved upon prior work. The findings about when LLM post-processing might actually *improve* privacy are significant and non-trivial.

I am not giving the paper a higher score because the results are largely restricted to word-level DP. However, the paper does expand previous work, offering insights into the practical use of LLMs in this context.

- **Score**: 7/10

### PAX-TS: Model-agnostic multi-granular explanations for time series forecasting via localized perturbations
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18982v1)
- **Authors**: Tim Kreuzer, Jelena Zdravkovic, Panagiotis Papapetrou
- **Abstract**: Time series forecasting has seen considerable improvement during the last years, with transformer models and large language models driving advancements of the state of the art. Modern forecasting models are generally opaque and do not provide explanations for their forecasts, while well-known post-hoc explainability methods like LIME are not suitable for the forecasting context. We propose PAX-TS, a model-agnostic post-hoc algorithm to explain time series forecasting models and their forecasts. Our method is based on localized input perturbations and results in multi-granular explanations. Further, it is able to characterize cross-channel correlations for multivariate time series forecasts. We clearly outline the algorithmic procedure behind PAX-TS, demonstrate it on a benchmark with 7 algorithms and 10 diverse datasets, compare it with two other state-of-the-art explanation algorithms, and present the different explanation types of the method. We found that the explanations of high-performing and low-performing algorithms differ on the same datasets, highlighting that the explanations of PAX-TS effectively capture a model's behavior. Based on time step correlation matrices resulting from the benchmark, we identify 6 classes of patterns that repeatedly occur across different datasets and algorithms. We found that the patterns are indicators of performance, with noticeable differences in forecasting error between the classes. Lastly, we outline a multivariate example where PAX-TS demonstrates how the forecasting model takes cross-channel correlations into account. With PAX-TS, time series forecasting models' mechanisms can be illustrated in different levels of detail, and its explanations can be used to answer practical questions on forecasts.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PAX-TS, a novel model-agnostic, post-hoc explainability technique for time series forecasting.  PAX-TS utilizes localized input perturbations to provide multi-granular explanations, ranging from high-level cross-channel correlations to detailed time-step importance. The method outlines a clear algorithmic procedure and presents empirical evaluations on a benchmark of diverse datasets and forecasting algorithms. It identifies temporal dependency patterns indicative of algorithm performance and provides a complexity analysis. A multivariate example demonstrates the use of PAX-TS for analyzing cross-channel correlations.  The paper compares PAX-TS to existing methods like TS-MULE and ShapTime, highlighting PAX-TS's superior capabilities in terms of granularity and applicability to multivariate data. The authors argue that PAX-TS helps answer practical end-user questions about time series forecasts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core idea of using localized perturbations for explaining time series forecasts isn't entirely new, as methods like TS-MULE also leverage perturbations. However, PAX-TS offers significant advancements in several key aspects, differentiating it from existing approaches.

*   **Multi-Granularity:**  The ability to provide explanations at different levels of granularity (time-step, summary statistics, cross-channel) is a valuable contribution. This allows end-users to tailor the explanation to their specific needs and level of expertise.  This goes beyond the segment-based explanations of methods like TS-MULE, offering greater detail.
*   **Focus on Practical Questions:**  The paper's grounding in practical end-user questions is a strength.  The motivation stemming from real-world challenges faced by practitioners makes the method more immediately relevant.
*   **Cross-Channel Analysis:** The handling of multivariate time series and the analysis of cross-channel correlations are significant.  This is an area where many existing explanation methods fall short, making PAX-TS a useful tool for complex forecasting scenarios. The ability to extract and visualize channel dependency patterns is innovative.
*   **Dependency Pattern Identification:** The identification and categorization of temporal dependency patterns (Diagonals, Last-Timestep, Bipolar Regions etc.) is a novel and valuable finding. The link these patterns to algorithm performance offers valuable insights into the behavior of different forecasting models.

**Significance:** The significance of PAX-TS lies in its potential to improve the interpretability and trustworthiness of time series forecasting models, particularly in real-world applications.

*   **Model Agnostic:** The model-agnostic nature of PAX-TS allows it to be applied to a wide range of forecasting algorithms, including state-of-the-art deep learning models.
*   **Improved Understanding:** By providing multi-granular explanations, PAX-TS can help domain experts understand how forecasting models make their predictions, leading to better informed decision-making.
*   **Performance Insights:**  The identification of temporal dependency patterns can guide the selection of appropriate forecasting models for specific datasets and tasks. This is particularly relevant to understand the dataset characteristics and which model is most suited.
*   **Reproducible Code:** The provision of reproducible code enhances the impact and adoption of PAX-TS.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Computational Cost:**  While the authors provide a complexity analysis, the computational cost associated with the method (especially with a large number of scale parameters |A| and large b) could be a limitation in some real-time applications, even with the reduction techniques described. More detailed benchmarks on inference time with different parameter configurations and dataset sizes would strengthen the analysis.
*   **Limited Comparison:** While the comparison to TS-MULE and ShapTime is helpful, a more in-depth comparison, particularly a quantitative comparison of explanation quality (if possible to define) would be beneficial. A direct evaluation metric for assessing explanation quality is lacking. The comparisons also appear to be based on visualizations, which is qualitative.
*   **Parameter Sensitivity:** There might be sensitivity to the choice of perturbation parameters (e.g., `w`, `s`, scale parameters `A`).  The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of how to select these parameters and the potential impact of different parameter settings on the resulting explanations.
*   **Real-World Case Studies:**  While the paper motivates the work with practical questions, it lacks a detailed real-world case study that demonstrates the application of PAX-TS to a specific forecasting problem and assesses its impact on decision-making.

**Justification for the Score:**

Given its clear novelty in multi-granularity, practical motivation, cross-channel analysis, dependency pattern identification, and model-agnostic nature, the paper represents a valuable contribution to the field of explainable time series forecasting. However, weaknesses regarding computational cost benchmarks, the need for direct qualitative metrics, sensitivity of the algorithm to parameters, and real-world application with decision-making make it so it should be scored lower than would otherwise seem.

### Enabling MoE on the Edge via Importance-Driven Expert Scheduling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18983v1)
- **Authors**: Guoying Zhu, Meng Li, Haipeng Dai, Xuechen Liu, Weijun Wang, Keran Li, Jun xiao, Ligeng Chen, Wei Wang
- **Abstract**: The Mixture of Experts (MoE) architecture has emerged as a key technique for scaling Large Language Models by activating only a subset of experts per query. Deploying MoE on consumer-grade edge hardware, however, is constrained by limited device memory, making dynamic expert offloading essential. Unlike prior work that treats offloading purely as a scheduling problem, we leverage expert importance to guide decisions, substituting low-importance activated experts with functionally similar ones already cached in GPU memory, thereby preserving accuracy. As a result, this design reduces memory usage and data transfer, while largely eliminating PCIe overhead. In addition, we introduce a scheduling policy that maximizes the reuse ratio of GPU-cached experts, further boosting efficiency. Extensive evaluations show that our approach delivers 48% lower decoding latency with over 60% expert cache hit rate, while maintaining nearly lossless accuracy.
- **Summary**: Okay, I've analyzed the provided paper, titled "Enabling MoE on the Edge via Importance-Driven Expert Scheduling". Here's a summary, followed by a critical evaluation and a score:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of deploying Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) Large Language Models (LLMs) on resource-constrained edge devices. The key idea is to leverage *expert importance* during the expert offloading process. Instead of treating all active experts equally, the approach prioritizes and substitutes less important (low-score) CPU-resident experts with similar, but GPU-resident (and therefore faster to access), experts.  This reduces PCIe transfers, saves on CPU computation, and significantly speeds up inference. It also introduces a scheduling policy that maximizes reuse of GPU-cached experts.  The authors implement a CPU-GPU-load pipeline system and evaluate it on several MoE models and workloads, demonstrating substantial reductions in decoding latency (TPOT) while maintaining accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using expert *importance* to guide the offloading and substitution process is a significant departure from existing approaches, which primarily focus on scheduling algorithms and data transfer optimization without considering the varying contributions of each expert. The integration of CPU as an auxiliary computation is also interesting. The *expert-cache router* is also a novel component.

*   **Significance:** Edge deployment of LLMs is crucial for numerous real-world applications (privacy, low latency, etc.). Making MoEs feasible on edge devices is a valuable contribution. The demonstrated reduction in TPOT is practically relevant, potentially enabling more responsive and usable LLM-powered applications on edge hardware. While other offloading strategies exist, this paper targets a very specific, but important issue (MoE on low-resource devices).

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenges and limitations of deploying MoE LLMs on edge devices, specifically related to the limited GPU memory and high latency associated with PCIe transfers.
    *   **Well-Defined Solution:** The proposed importance-driven expert scheduling approach is well-motivated and clearly described. The design of the expert-cache router, prefetching, and CPU-load balancing is logical.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments are thorough, covering multiple MoE models (DeepseekMoE, XVERSE, Qwen2), hardware configurations (3080Ti, 4060Ti, A6000), and diverse workloads. The ablation studies effectively dissect the contribution of each component. The accuracy analysis justifies the small trade-off involved.
    *   **Practical Focus:** The emphasis on reducing TPOT makes the work relevant and valuable for real-world applications.
    *   **Good writing:** The paper is clearly written and well-organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on DeepSeekMoE Architecture:** The paper explicitly mentions that it evaluates models compatible with the DeepSeekMoE architecture. While they claim other models can be supported, a more general solution would be better.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Alternative Importance Metrics:** The paper uses the router's gate score as the importance metric.  Exploring other potential metrics (e.g., gradient norms, output sensitivity) could have strengthened the approach.
    *   **Limited Comparison to Other Online Serving Methods**: The comparison focuses on CPU vs. GPU and lacks comparison of other online serving methods that might have already implemented ideas that have some overlap with expert importance.
    *   **Incremental vs. Revolutionary:** While the combination of techniques provides a clear benefit, many of the steps that have been taken are already used.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence future research on edge deployment of LLMs. It highlights the importance of considering expert characteristics beyond simple activity levels. The ideas could be extended to other model architectures and hardware platforms. Specifically, the expert-cache router could be implemented with other architectures to achieve further benefits.
*   **Missing Figure**: Figure 7 seems to be missing from the document.

**Score:**

Score: 7

**Justification:**

The paper presents a solid and well-executed approach to a relevant problem. The novelty lies in the specific combination of expert importance, caching, prefetching, and CPU-GPU pipelining tailored for MoE LLMs on edge devices, rather than a single, groundbreaking invention. The comprehensive evaluation and practical focus are significant strengths. However, the dependence on a specific model architecture and the lack of exploration into additional importance metrics are notable limitations. Overall, it represents a significant incremental advancement with practical implications, warranting a score of 7.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Automatic Prompt Optimization with Prompt Distillation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.18992v1)
- **Authors**: Viktor N. Zhuravlev, Artur R. Khairullin, Ernest A. Dyagin, Alena N. Sitkina, Nikita I. Kulin
- **Abstract**: Autoprompting is the process of automatically selecting optimized prompts for language models, which is gaining popularity due to the rapid development of prompt engineering driven by extensive research in the field of large language models (LLMs). This paper presents DistillPrompt -- a novel autoprompting method based on large language models that employs a multi-stage integration of task-specific information into prompts using training data. DistillPrompt utilizes distillation, compression, and aggregation operations to explore the prompt space more thoroughly. The method was tested on different datasets for text classification and generation tasks using the t-lite-instruct-0.1 language model. The results demonstrate a significant average improvement (e.g., 20.12% across the entire dataset compared to Grips) in key metrics over existing methods in the field, establishing DistillPrompt as one of the most effective non-gradient approaches in autoprompting.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DistillPrompt, a novel non-gradient-based autoprompting method for large language models (LLMs).  DistillPrompt iteratively refines prompts through a multi-stage process: generating prompt candidates, embedding task-relevant examples, compressing instructions, aggregating candidates, and creating new variations. The method is evaluated on text classification and generation tasks, demonstrating improved performance compared to existing prompting techniques and non-gradient autoprompting approaches like Grips and Protegi, particularly when using the t-lite-instruct-0.1 LLM. The core idea is complex prompt distillation through multiple stages.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in the *specific combination* of techniques used in DistillPrompt. While individual components such as prompt generation, example embedding, and compression have been explored separately, the multi-stage, iterative integration of these methods, termed "prompt distillation," is a distinguishing factor. The use of the LLM itself at each stage of prompt manipulation (generation, distillation) is also interesting. However, the individual pieces themselves are not revolutionary.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the practical improvements demonstrated on several NLP tasks. Outperforming existing non-gradient methods like Grips is valuable, especially given the increasing interest in accessible and efficient prompting techniques. The modular structure allows it to potentially be modified and adapted to different types of tasks. The authors provide an open-source library (CoolPrompt framework library), which would assist other researchers to easily replicate and extend their findings.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Comprehensive evaluation across multiple datasets (classification and generation).
    *   Clear description of the DistillPrompt methodology and its individual steps.
    *   Significant performance gains compared to baselines (particularly Grips) especially given no need for gradient descent.
    *   Code available for reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited scope for LLMs: The paper uses the t-lite-instruct-0.1 model, which is not the most modern or powerful LLM currently available. It's possible the effectiveness of DistillPrompt is highly dependent on the specific model chosen. The paper would benefit from an ablation study investigating the importance of individual stages and hyperparameter sensitivity (e.g., varying N or K).
    *   Incremental improvement: While the performance increase is meaningful, it is not ground breaking or disruptive. This is an improvement on existing techniques, but not a paradigm shift.
    *   Limited exploration beyond the proposed method: The paper does not analyze why DistillPrompt works or explore the types of prompts it generates.

*   **Impact:** The paper could have a moderate impact. It provides a practical, relatively accessible method for improving prompt engineering, potentially benefiting researchers and practitioners working with LLMs. The open-source nature facilitates adoption and further research. However, its impact is somewhat limited by its reliance on a specific, less-powerful LLM in the current rapidly changing LLM landscape. The relative simplicity of the underlying approach also suggests it could be quickly superseded by more advanced methods.

**Justification for Score:**

DistillPrompt introduces a novel combination of techniques that improves prompt engineering. However, the approach is incremental, and its true significance is limited by dependence on a less powerful model and limited scope of analysis.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### AI Models Exceed Individual Human Accuracy in Predicting Everyday Social Norms
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19004v1)
- **Authors**: Pontus Strimling, Simon Karlsson, Irina Vartanova, Kimmo Eriksson
- **Abstract**: A fundamental question in cognitive science concerns how social norms are acquired and represented. While humans typically learn norms through embodied social experience, we investigated whether large language models can achieve sophisticated norm understanding through statistical learning alone. Across two studies, we systematically evaluated multiple AI systems' ability to predict human social appropriateness judgments for 555 everyday scenarios by examining how closely they predicted the average judgment compared to each human participant. In Study 1, GPT-4.5's accuracy in predicting the collective judgment on a continuous scale exceeded that of every human participant (100th percentile). Study 2 replicated this, with Gemini 2.5 Pro outperforming 98.7% of humans, GPT-5 97.8%, and Claude Sonnet 4 96.0%. Despite this predictive power, all models showed systematic, correlated errors. These findings demonstrate that sophisticated models of social cognition can emerge from statistical learning over linguistic data alone, challenging strong versions of theories emphasizing the exclusive necessity of embodied experience for cultural competence. The systematic nature of AI limitations across different architectures indicates potential boundaries of pattern-based social understanding, while the models' ability to outperform nearly all individual humans in this predictive task suggests that language serves as a remarkably rich repository for cultural knowledge transmission.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to predict human judgments of social appropriateness in everyday scenarios. Across two studies, the authors compared LLMs' predictions against a dataset of human ratings for 555 common situations. The key findings are: 1) LLMs (GPT-4.5, Gemini 2.5 Pro, GPT-5, Claude Sonnet 4) demonstrated a strong correlation with the average human ratings, explaining a large percentage of the variance; 2) The LLMs' accuracy in predicting the average social appropriateness judgment often exceeded that of individual human participants; 3)  LLMs exhibited systematic, correlated errors, suggesting limitations in their social understanding related to contextual ambiguity, training data biases, and context-dependent valence shifts. The authors conclude that LLMs can achieve sophisticated norm understanding through statistical learning alone, challenging theories emphasizing embodied experience. However, they acknowledge the existence of boundaries to this type of social understanding.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel approach to evaluating LLMs' social reasoning capabilities. Prior work has often focused on moral dilemmas or binary classifications of social behaviors, benchmarking AI against predetermined "correct" answers. This paper, in contrast, focuses on the nuanced and continuous spectrum of everyday social appropriateness, comparing LLM performance to the natural variation in human judgment at an individual level. The shift from benchmarking against a 'ground truth' to comparing against human performance variability, is indeed novel.

**Significance:** The findings have important implications for theories of social cognition and computational modeling. Showing that LLMs can outperform most individual humans in predicting social norms solely from statistical learning raises serious questions about the necessity of embodied experience for developing social competence, a core tenet of some schools of cognitive psychology. This challenges strong embodiment theories. Furthermore, the paper provides a methodological innovation (individual-level benchmarking) and identifies specific types of scenarios where LLMs struggle (contextual ambiguity, training data biases, and valence shifts). This is crucial for future research and developing more robust social intelligence in AI.

**Strengths:**
*   **Methodological rigor:**  The study employs a large-scale dataset, multiple LLMs, and careful prompt engineering. The use of multiple queries per scenario helps mitigate stochastic variability in LLM outputs. They also perform supplementary analyses to rule out alternative explanations.
*   **Novel benchmarking approach:** The individual-level performance comparison is a significant improvement over previous methods, providing a more nuanced understanding of LLMs' capabilities.
*   **Identification of limitations:** The analysis of AI-human divergences provides valuable insights into the specific areas where LLMs fall short, guiding future research.
*   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and organized, with clear explanations of the methods, results, and implications.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Limited cultural scope:** The study focuses solely on U.S. social norms. The generalizability of the findings to other cultures is an open question. The authors acknowledge this, but it's an important limitation.
*   **Meta-cognitive vs. First-Person Judgment:**  The LLMs were explicitly prompted to predict the *average* human rating, while the human participants offered their *own* rating. Although the authors provide a rationale for comparing these, this is not a perfect comparison. This is not a 'fatal' flaw by any means, but it should be acknowledged that the AI task is perhaps more computationally amenable since it specifically frames social cognition as predictive rather than reflexive.
*   **Reliance on retired Models:** While GPT-4.5 was SOTA when the first part of the study was conducted, comparing it with current models would be a better comparison
*   **Correlation isn't causation:** It's impossible to say that performance is the result of 'social understanding', since it's possible that there is something else going on.

**Justification for the Score:**

Despite the limitations, the paper's strengths significantly outweigh its weaknesses. The novel benchmarking approach, the clear demonstration of LLMs' capabilities in predicting social norms, and the identification of specific areas where LLMs struggle make this a significant contribution to the field. This work will likely stimulate further research on the computational modeling of social cognition and the role of embodied experience in social learning. While some of its findings might be constrained to Western cultures, the study provides a strong foundation for future cross-cultural investigations. The limitations that the study makes can also be attributed to challenges in LLM research, and does not necessarily detract from the novelty of the paper

### Sense of Self and Time in Borderline Personality. A Comparative Robustness Study with Generative AI
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19008v1)
- **Authors**: Marcin Moskalewicz, Anna Sterna, Marek Pokropski, Paula Flores
- **Abstract**: This study examines the capacity of large language models (LLMs) to support phenomenological qualitative analysis of first-person experience in Borderline Personality Disorder (BPD), understood as a disorder of temporality and selfhood. Building on a prior human-led thematic analysis of 24 inpatients' life-story interviews, we compared three LLMs (OpenAI GPT-4o, Google Gemini 2.5 Pro, Anthropic Claude Opus 4) prompted to mimic the interpretative style of the original investigators. The models were evaluated with blinded and non-blinded expert judges in phenomenology and clinical psychology. Assessments included semantic congruence, Jaccard coefficients, and multidimensional validity ratings (credibility, coherence, substantiveness, and groundness in data). Results showed variable overlap with the human analysis, from 0 percent in GPT to 42 percent in Claude and 58 percent in Gemini, and a low Jaccard coefficient (0.21-0.28). However, the models recovered themes omitted by humans. Gemini's output most closely resembled the human analysis, with validity scores significantly higher than GPT and Claude (p < 0.0001), and was judged as human by blinded experts. All scores strongly correlated (R > 0.78) with the quantity of text and words per theme, highlighting both the variability and potential of AI-augmented thematic analysis to mitigate human interpretative bias.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This study investigates the effectiveness of Large Language Models (LLMs) in analyzing first-person narratives related to Borderline Personality Disorder (BPD), focusing on themes of temporality and selfhood. Three LLMs (GPT-4o, Gemini 2.5 Pro, and Claude Opus 4) were tasked with replicating a prior human-led thematic analysis of life-story interviews. The models' outputs were evaluated by expert judges (blinded and non-blinded) using metrics such as semantic congruence, Jaccard coefficients, and validity ratings related to credibility, coherence, and groundness.  The study found variable semantic overlap between the AI and human analysis, with Gemini performing best and being occasionally mistaken for human output by blinded evaluators. While the LLMs identified some themes missed by humans, overall, the human analysis still surpassed the AI in validity scores. The paper concludes that AI-augmented thematic analysis shows promise, especially in mitigating human interpretative bias, but requires careful human oversight.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Application:**  The study explores a relatively new application of LLMs: the qualitative analysis of complex psychological narratives, specifically within the nuanced domain of BPD phenomenology. This area of AI application is still developing, and any well-designed study contributes to understanding its potential and limitations.
    *   **Rigorous Methodology:** The study employs a mixed-methods approach involving both quantitative (Jaccard coefficient, statistical comparison of validity scores) and qualitative (expert evaluations, thematic analysis comparison) metrics.  Blinding procedures were used to mitigate bias in the assessment of AI-generated outputs.
    *   **Focus on Interpretative Bias:** The explicit aim of mitigating human interpretative bias is a significant strength. This addresses a key challenge in qualitative research and opens avenues for using AI as a tool for enhancing objectivity. The study highlights how LLMs might identify themes missed by human analysts.
    *   **Detailed Prompt Engineering:** The paper provides a decent description of the prompt engineering used, including the use of persona prompting and the CO-STAR framework.  This is crucial for reproducibility and understanding the impact of prompt design on the LLMs' outputs.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in AI Techniques:**  The study relies on relatively standard prompting methods for LLMs. It doesn't explore cutting-edge techniques in prompt engineering, few-shot learning, or fine-tuning that might significantly improve the models' performance. The choice of baseline LLMs isn't explicitly justified, and there's no exploration of more specialized or open-source models. It uses default hyperparameter setting which is a further weakness.
    *   **Lack of Theoretical Depth:**  While the paper is grounded in phenomenological theory, the analysis of the LLMs' outputs does not significantly advance or challenge existing understandings of BPD. The focus is more on methodological comparison than on generating new insights into the condition itself.
    *   **Modest Impact on Qualitative Research:** The paper demonstrates potential for AI assistance, but the overall performance of the LLMs suggests that human expertise remains essential. It raises questions about the level of resources/energy of human input/verification required to use these models, relative to existing methods, and whether the benefits outweigh the costs. It does not describe how to make thematic analysis more cost/time effective and does not prove that AI is a good tool given the time/energy that needs to be invested.
    *   **Small Sample Size and Limited Generalizability:**  The study uses a relatively small sample of BPD patient narratives, which limits the generalizability of the findings. Further research with larger and more diverse datasets is needed. Also the description of the original dataset is vague: *early stage of long-term inpatient treatment program*, *range 2-20 weeks*: we don't know how advanced in the treatment they were, nor how the treatment focused on.
    *   **Overestimation of the value of the AI contribution?**: authors make a case for AI help when the evaluation points out the lack of depth, and the fact that it just restates facts without really thinking or analysing.

**Significance and Influence:**

The study has the potential to influence the application of AI in qualitative psychological research. It provides empirical evidence for both the strengths and limitations of LLMs in this context. It highlights the importance of careful prompt engineering, expert oversight, and a critical assessment of the models' outputs.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite its strengths in methodology and focus on interpretative bias, the paper's limited novelty in AI techniques, lack of deep theoretical contribution, and modest impact on qualitative research warrant a relatively conservative score. The study provides a valuable snapshot of the current capabilities of LLMs in qualitative analysis, but it does not represent a breakthrough that will fundamentally change the field.

**Score: 5**

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 5/10

### STDiff: A State Transition Diffusion Framework for Time Series Imputation in Industrial Systems
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19011v1)
- **Authors**: Gary Simethy, Daniel Ortiz-Arroyo, Petar Durdevic
- **Abstract**: Most deep learning methods for imputing missing values treat the task as completing patterns within a fixed time window. This assumption often fails in industrial systems, where dynamics are driven by control actions, are highly non-stationary, and can experience long, uninterrupted gaps. We propose STDiff, which reframes imputation as learning how the system evolves from one state to the next. STDiff uses a conditional denoising diffusion model with a causal bias aligned to control theory, generating missing values step-by-step based on the most recent known state and relevant control or environmental inputs. On a public wastewater treatment dataset with simulated missing blocks, STDiff consistently achieves the lowest errors, with its advantage increasing for longer gaps. On a raw industrial dataset with substantial real gaps, it produces trajectories that remain dynamically plausible, in contrast to window-based models that tend to flatten or over-smooth. These results support dynamics-aware, explicitly conditioned imputation as a robust approach for industrial time series, and we discuss computational trade-offs and extensions to broader domains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "STDiff: A State Transition Diffusion Framework for Time Series Imputation in Industrial Systems":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces STDiff, a novel framework for imputing missing values in industrial time series data. It addresses the limitations of existing window-based imputation methods that struggle with long, contiguous gaps and non-stationary data typical of industrial systems. STDiff reframes imputation as a probabilistic state transition modeling problem, learning how the system evolves from one state to the next using a conditional denoising diffusion model. The model incorporates a causal bias aligned with control theory and explicitly conditions on control inputs and exogenous variables. The authors demonstrate STDiff's superior performance against several baselines on wastewater treatment plant (WWTP) datasets with both synthetic and real missing data, particularly in handling long gaps and maintaining dynamic plausibility.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Problem Relevance:** Addresses a critical challenge in industrial time series analysis, where missing data frequently occurs in long, non-stationary chunks, rendering window-based approaches ineffective.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The state transition diffusion model provides a strong alternative to existing imputation techniques, particularly for dynamical systems. The explicit conditioning on control inputs and exogenous variables is a key differentiator that allows the model to capture causal dependencies.
    *   **Strong Empirical Validation:** Thorough evaluation on relevant WWTP datasets, demonstrating improved accuracy and dynamic plausibility compared to strong baselines. The use of both synthetic and real data adds to the credibility of the results.
    *   **Causal Inductive Bias:** The formulation explicitly respects the causal direction of control-driven systems.
    *   **Clear Articulation:** The paper is well-written, clearly explaining the methodology and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** The reverse diffusion process requires a large number of steps (T=1000), leading to potentially high inference costs, especially for long gaps. While the authors acknowledge this and suggest potential optimizations, this remains a practical limitation.
    *   **Markov Assumption:** The framework relies on a first-order Markov assumption, which may not hold for all industrial systems with longer-term dependencies. While they suggest extending this with an encoder, it is not currently explored.
    *   **Input Quality Dependence:** The model’s performance depends on the quality of control and exogenous inputs. While the authors mitigate this by injecting dropouts, it remains a vulnerability if these inputs are consistently missing or unreliable.
    *   **Limited Domain Validation:** The primary focus is on wastewater treatment plants. While relevant, extending the validation to other industrial domains would strengthen the paper's generalizability.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The paper's novelty lies in the application of conditional denoising diffusion models to the time series imputation problem, particularly with an emphasis on state transitions and causal conditioning.  While diffusion models have been used for time series imputation before (e.g., CSDI), STDiff stands out due to its deliberate framing as a dynamics learning problem and integration of control variables. The explicit causal bias is a strong contribution.
    *   The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the robustness and accuracy of industrial control systems by providing a more reliable imputation method for missing data. The ability to handle long gaps is crucial in many industrial applications, where data availability can be intermittent.

**Justification:**

The paper offers a well-formulated and empirically validated solution to a significant problem in industrial time series analysis. The state-transition perspective and the use of diffusion models with explicit causal conditioning is a notable contribution. While the computational cost and reliance on input quality are limitations, the paper's strengths in terms of problem relevance, novel approach, and strong validation outweigh these weaknesses. The paper demonstrates promising results and opens up interesting avenues for future research in dynamics-aware imputation.

- **Score**: 7/10

### MovieCORE: COgnitive REasoning in Movies
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19026v1)
- **Authors**: Gueter Josmy Faure, Min-Hung Chen, Jia-Fong Yeh, Ying Cheng, Hung-Ting Su, Yung-Hao Tang, Shang-Hong Lai, Winston H. Hsu
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces MovieCORE, a novel video question answering (VQA) dataset designed to probe deeper cognitive understanding of movie content. Unlike existing datasets that focus on surface-level comprehension, MovieCORE emphasizes questions that engage System-2 thinking while remaining specific to the video material. We present an innovative agentic brainstorming approach, utilizing multiple large language models (LLMs) as thought agents to generate and refine high-quality question-answer pairs. To evaluate dataset quality, we develop a set of cognitive tests assessing depth, thought-provocation potential, and syntactic complexity. We also propose a comprehensive evaluation scheme for assessing VQA model performance on deeper cognitive tasks. To address the limitations of existing video-language models (VLMs), we introduce an agentic enhancement module, Agentic Choice Enhancement (ACE), which improves model reasoning capabilities post-training by up to 25%. Our work contributes to advancing movie understanding in AI systems and provides valuable insights into the capabilities and limitations of current VQA models when faced with more challenging, nuanced questions about cinematic content. Our project page, dataset and code can be found at https://joslefaure.github.io/assets/html/moviecore.html.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and critical evaluation of the "MovieCORE: Cognitive Reasoning in Movies" paper based on the provided text.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces MovieCORE, a novel video question answering (VQA) dataset designed to assess deeper cognitive understanding of movie content, going beyond surface-level comprehension.  It addresses the lack of existing VQA datasets that truly probe "System-2" thinking processes when interpreting cinematic material. The authors use an innovative "agentic brainstorming" approach, employing multiple LLMs as specialized "thought agents" to generate and refine high-quality question-answer pairs. They also develop a set of cognitive tests to evaluate the depth, thought-provoking nature, and syntactic complexity of the dataset. The paper then proposes a comprehensive evaluation scheme for assessing VQA model performance, and introduces an "agentic enhancement module" (ACE) that improves reasoning capabilities in existing video-language models (VLMs), demonstrating a performance boost of up to 25%.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The paper demonstrates significant novelty in several areas:

*   **Dataset Focus:** The primary novelty lies in the dataset's specific focus on eliciting System-2, cognitive reasoning.  Existing movie-based VQA datasets tend to focus on factual information or surface-level comprehension. MovieCORE, in contrast, attempts to address the "how" and "why" questions that are crucial for profound understanding, embracing the inherent subjectivity.
*   **Agentic Brainstorming:** The agentic brainstorming approach for QA generation is a strong point. It moves beyond simple automation and leverages the collaborative capabilities of multiple LLMs to create richer, more nuanced questions and answers. This approach is not commonly seen in dataset creation and it addresses the challenge of generating thought-provoking questions.
*   **Agentic Choice Enhancement (ACE):** ACE, as a post-training plugin to improve reasoning capabilities in existing VLMs, offers a practical and efficient way to enhance performance without requiring extensive retraining.

**Significance:**

*   **Addressing a Gap:** MovieCORE directly tackles a recognized gap in the field of video understanding. The ability to reason deeply about cinematic content is essential for truly intelligent systems, and MovieCORE provides a valuable benchmark for measuring progress in this area.
*   **Practical Application:** The ACE module showcases the practical relevance of the work. It offers a tangible approach to improve the reasoning capabilities of existing VLMs on more challenging tasks.
*   **Insights into VLM Limitations:** The paper's evaluation of current VQA models reveals critical insights into their limitations when faced with complex, nuanced questions, opening up avenues for future research and model development.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing VQA datasets and the need for a resource that promotes deeper cognitive reasoning.
*   **Comprehensive Methodology:** The authors present a well-defined and thorough methodology for dataset creation, evaluation, and model enhancement.
*   **Quantitative and Qualitative Results:** The inclusion of both quantitative metrics (parse tree depth, F-K grade score, Bloom's Taxonomy levels, model performance on various dimensions) and qualitative examples strengthens the paper's arguments.
*   **Strong Empirical Evidence:** The experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of ACE and the challenges faced by existing VLMs on MovieCORE provide compelling evidence of the dataset's value and the limitations it reveals.
*   **Addressing the subjectivity and biases of LLMs** The authors acknowledge the bias issues related to the LLM scoring and design the framework accordingly to diminish the bias impact.
*  **Providing full detail of the process** The supplementary material and evaluation code are a great contribution to the scientific community.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Dependency on LLMs**: The evaluation is done with LLM based automated metrics. Since LLMs can hallucinate and are prone to generating biases, this type of evaluation can be questionable. Addressing those issues, the authors include human in the loop validations in the evaluation workflow which is a major strenght.
*   **Limited Human Verification:** While the authors include human verification, it only covers a small subset of the dataset. Expanding human verification would further enhance the dataset's reliability.
*   **Genre Coverage:** The paper acknowledges that the dataset's genre coverage may be constrained due to its origin in the MovieChat-1k collection. Addressing this limitation would improve the dataset's generalizability.
* LLM based annotations may contain bias The nature of generating a datset through LLM prompts may also introduce bias, such as gender bias or race bias. This must be carefully addressed.

**Potential Influence:**

MovieCORE has the potential to significantly influence the field of video understanding by:

*   **Driving VQA Research:** It will serve as a challenging benchmark for developing VQA models with improved cognitive reasoning capabilities.
*   **Promoting System-2 Thinking in AI:** It can encourage research into AI systems that can engage in more deliberate, analytical thinking processes.
*   **Inspiring New Dataset Creation Methodologies:** The agentic brainstorming approach could inspire new methods for creating high-quality datasets in other domains.

**Justification for Score:**

The MovieCORE paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of video understanding. The dataset's focus on cognitive reasoning, the innovative agentic brainstorming approach, and the ACE module all represent valuable advancements. While the limitations related to human verification and genre coverage are important to acknowledge, they do not diminish the paper's overall impact. Given its potential to drive future research and improve the capabilities of VQA models, a high score is warranted.

### When recalling in-context, Transformers are not SSMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19029v1)
- **Authors**: Destiny Okpekpe, Antonio Orvieto
- **Abstract**: Despite the advantageous subquadratic complexity of modern recurrent deep learning models -- such as state-space models (SSMs) -- recent studies have highlighted their potential shortcomings compared to transformers on reasoning and memorization tasks. In this paper, we dive deeper into one of such benchmarks: associative recall (AR), which has been shown to correlate well with language modeling performance, and inspect in detail the effects of scaling and optimization issues in recently proposed token mixing strategies. We first demonstrate that, unlike standard transformers, the choice of learning rate plays a critical role in the performance of modern recurrent models: an issue that can severely affect reported performance in previous works and suggests further research is needed to stabilize training. Next, we show that recurrent and attention-based models exhibit contrasting benefits when scaling in width as opposed to depth, with attention being notably unable to solve AR when limited to a single layer. We then further inspect 1-layer transformers, revealing that despite their poor performance, their training dynamics surprisingly resemble the formation of induction heads, a phenomenon previously observed only in their 2-layer counterparts. Finally, through architectural ablations, we study how components affects Transformer and Mamba's performance and optimization stability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the performance of Transformer-based and State-Space Model (SSM)-based architectures on the associative recall (AR) task, a benchmark for reasoning and memorization.  It challenges the notion that SSMs are inherently less capable than Transformers on such tasks. The authors demonstrate that optimization plays a crucial role, particularly the selection of an appropriate learning rate, which can significantly affect SSM performance.  They also explore the impact of scaling width versus depth in both types of models, finding that recurrent models benefit more from increased width, while attention models require at least two layers to perform well.  The paper analyzes the training dynamics of single-layer models, uncovering similarities to induction head formation in attention mechanisms, and performs ablation studies on Mamba to understand the impact of its architectural components. Finally, it explores architectural variants of Mamba, such as DeltaNet.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The paper's novelty lies in its detailed investigation of the factors affecting SSM performance on the AR task.  While the AR task itself is not new, the systematic analysis of optimization (specifically, the learning rate sensitivity of SSMs), scaling behavior, and training dynamics, provides valuable insights. The ablation studies of the Mamba architecture, and their implication that the gating and convolutional components are not the only source of its performance boost, is another contribution. The finding that attention models attempt to form induction heads even in single-layer architectures, although ultimately failing to leverage them, is also a novel observation.

*   **Significance:**  The significance of this work stems from its potential to improve the understanding and utilization of SSMs.  The highlighting of learning rate sensitivity is a practical takeaway for researchers and practitioners working with these models. The insights into width/depth scaling and the impact of architectural components can guide the design of more effective SSM architectures. The exploration of DeltaNet's improved stability opens avenues for further research. More broadly, the paper contributes to the ongoing debate about the relative strengths and weaknesses of attention-based and recurrent models, emphasizing the importance of accounting for optimization when making such comparisons.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined research questions and a clear focus on understanding the performance gap between Transformers and SSMs on AR.
    *   Systematic experimental design, with careful control of hyperparameters and multiple seeds to ensure robustness.
    *   Detailed analysis of training dynamics, providing insights into the internal workings of the models.
    *   Ablation studies that effectively isolate the impact of different architectural components.
    *   Clear and concise presentation of results, with informative figures.
    * It addresses a significant confounder for future model evaluations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper primarily focuses on the AR task, which, while a useful benchmark, is still a synthetic problem.  It would be beneficial to see how these findings generalize to more realistic NLP tasks.
    *   The number of runs could be expanded. The reported max-min errors are large.
    *   The ablation study could be further expanded. For example, there is no clear analysis of the number of parameters of each model or a correlation between those and performance.
    *   While the paper identifies potential optimization problems and DeltaNet's stability, it does not provide a definitive solution or a comprehensive explanation for the stability differences beyond pointing to Householder matrices. More detailed analysis (e.g., spectral analysis of the weight matrices) could strengthen this conclusion.

*   **Impact:**  The paper has the potential to influence future research in the design and training of SSMs. By highlighting the importance of optimization and providing practical guidance for hyperparameter tuning, it can help researchers to unlock the full potential of these models.

**Score:** 7

**Rationale:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field by providing a detailed empirical analysis of the factors affecting SSM performance on the AR task. The findings are novel and relevant, providing practical insights for researchers working with these models. However, the scope of the study is limited to a synthetic task, and the investigation of optimization issues could be more in-depth. Therefore, while the paper demonstrates a reasonable level of novelty and has the potential to influence future research, it is not groundbreaking enough to warrant a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Investigating Advanced Reasoning of Large Language Models via Black-Box Interaction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19035v1)
- **Authors**: Congchi Yin, Tianyi Wu, Yankai Shu, Alex Gu, Yunhan Wang, Jun Shao, Xun Jiang, Piji Li
- **Abstract**: Existing tasks fall short in evaluating reasoning ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) in an interactive, unknown environment. This deficiency leads to the isolated assessment of deductive, inductive, and abductive reasoning, neglecting the integrated reasoning process that is indispensable for humans discovery of real world. We introduce a novel evaluation paradigm, \textit{black-box interaction}, to tackle this challenge. A black-box is defined by a hidden function that maps a specific set of inputs to outputs. LLMs are required to unravel the hidden function behind the black-box by interacting with it in given exploration turns, and reasoning over observed input-output pairs. Leveraging this idea, we build the \textsc{Oracle} benchmark which comprises 6 types of black-box task and 96 black-boxes. 19 modern LLMs are benchmarked. o3 ranks first in 5 of the 6 tasks, achieving over 70\% accuracy on most easy black-boxes. But it still struggles with some hard black-box tasks, where its average performance drops below 40\%. Further analysis indicates a universal difficulty among LLMs: They lack the high-level planning capability to develop efficient and adaptive exploration strategies for hypothesis refinement.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Investigating Advanced Reasoning of Large Language Models Via Black-Box Interaction":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel evaluation paradigm called "black-box interaction" to assess the integrated reasoning abilities of Large Language Models (LLMs). This paradigm challenges LLMs to unravel the hidden function behind a black box by interacting with it through a limited number of input-output queries. The authors create the ORACLE benchmark consisting of 96 black-boxes across six different tasks, including Code Intent Inference, Circuit Rule Inference, Physics System Inference, Encryption Rule Inference, Interactive Puzzle Inference, and Game Strategy Inference. They evaluate 19 leading LLMs on the benchmark, finding that while some models achieve high accuracy on simpler black-boxes, performance significantly drops on more complex tasks. The analysis reveals a key weakness of LLMs: a lack of high-level planning capabilities necessary for developing efficient and adaptive exploration strategies for hypothesis refinement.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The black-box interaction paradigm is a significant contribution. It moves beyond isolated assessments of deductive, inductive, and abductive reasoning by forcing LLMs to engage in a more holistic reasoning cycle. This interactive approach is more aligned with how humans discover and learn in real-world, unknown environments. The automated agentic framework for black-box construction also enables easy scaling of the benchmark which is another contribution.

*   **Significance:** Existing reasoning benchmarks often fall short by not placing LLMs in truly unknown, interactive settings. ORACLE offers an evaluation task that is designed to tackle integrated reasoning abilities more holistically.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Paradigm Shift:** Introduces a compelling new way to evaluate LLMs' reasoning.
    *   **Benchmark Design:** The ORACLE benchmark provides a diverse and challenging set of tasks. The black-box paradigm is well-motivated and the automated construction framework ensures scalability.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper provides a thorough evaluation of numerous LLMs, both proprietary and open-weight.
    *   **In-depth Analysis:** Identifies a key limitation of LLMs: the inability to develop efficient exploration strategies. The analysis using the two settings of CRI and ERI is well-justified.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** Evaluating LLMs, particularly expensive ones, is computationally intensive, limiting the scope of the study. The computational cost may also have limited the ability to perform rigorous statistical analysis of the ORACLE benchmark.
    *   **Scope:** The study is primarily focused on evaluating LLMs within the ORACLE benchmark itself. The evaluation of LLM-based agents utilizing black-box interaction is reserved for future research.
    *   **Complexity of Black-Boxes**: While the authors made the paradigm simple, the task is still quite complex for current LLMs, potentially conflating reasoning with other abilities such as long-context understanding.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   The black-box interaction paradigm will likely influence future reasoning benchmark design, pushing the field towards more interactive and realistic evaluations.
    *   The identified weakness in LLMs' exploration strategies provides a clear direction for future research on improving LLMs' reasoning abilities.
    *   The automated black-box construction framework provides a pathway for creating a wide range of adaptable reasoning tasks.

**Score: 8**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a well-designed evaluation methodology to assess reasoning abilities for LLMs. The automated framework allows for easy expansion of the benchmark. The in-depth analysis revealed key weaknesses of LLMs. The paper has some limitation. Firstly, there is a limited capacity to test more LLMs for the study due to the heavy computing cost. There could be room to explore more complicated black-boxes that require a combination of different reasoning skills, pushing the model to reason on different tasks at the same time. Therefore, a score of 8 is assigned.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### Of the People, By the Algorithm: How AI Transforms Democratic Representation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.19036v1)
- **Authors**: Yuval Rymon
- **Abstract**: This review examines how AI technologies are transforming democratic representation, focusing on citizen participation and algorithmic decision-making. The analysis reveals that AI technologies are reshaping democratic processes in fundamental ways: enabling mass-scale deliberation, changing how citizens access and engage with political information, and transforming how representatives make and implement decisions. While AI offers unprecedented opportunities for enhancing democratic participation and governance efficiency, it also presents significant challenges to democratic legitimacy and accountability. Social media platforms' AI-driven algorithms currently mediate much political discourse, creating concerns about information manipulation and privacy. Large Language Models introduce both epistemic challenges and potential tools for improving democratic dialogue. The emergence of Mass Online Deliberation platforms suggests possibilities for scaling up meaningful citizen participation, while Algorithmic Decision-Making systems promise more efficient policy implementation but face limitations in handling complex political trade-offs. As these systems become prevalent, representatives may assume the role of architects of automated decision frameworks, responsible for guiding the translation of politically contested concepts into technical parameters and metrics. Advanced deliberation platforms offering real-time insights into citizen preferences will challenge traditional representative independence and discretion to interpret public will. The institutional integration of these participation mechanisms requires frameworks that balance the benefits with democratic stability through hybrid systems weighting different forms of democratic expression.
- **Summary**: Okay, I can provide a summary and evaluation of the paper based on the OCR output.

**Summary:**

The paper "Of the People, By the Algorithm: How AI Transforms Democratic Representation" reviews the ways in which artificial intelligence technologies are transforming democratic processes, focusing on citizen participation and algorithmic decision-making.  It argues that AI is fundamentally reshaping democracy by enabling mass-scale deliberation, changing citizen access to political information, and transforming how representatives make decisions. While AI offers opportunities for enhancing participation and governance, it also poses challenges to democratic legitimacy and accountability.  The paper discusses the impact of social media algorithms, large language models (LLMs), and mass online deliberation (MOD) platforms.  It highlights potential issues like information manipulation, privacy concerns, epistemic challenges associated with LLMs, and the limitations of algorithmic decision-making in handling complex political trade-offs.  The paper suggests that representatives may increasingly become architects of automated decision frameworks and must balance algorithmic efficiency with democratic values. It concludes that institutional integration of digital participation requires frameworks that balance the benefits of AI with democratic stability through hybrid systems that appropriately weight different forms of democratic expression.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The paper offers a broad overview of the various ways AI is impacting democratic representation, encompassing citizen participation, information access, deliberation, and decision-making.
    *   **Balanced Perspective:** The paper avoids naive technological optimism by acknowledging both the potential benefits and the significant risks associated with AI in democracy. It critically examines challenges like bias, manipulation, and the erosion of accountability.
    *   **Well-structured Analysis:** The paper is organized logically, addressing key areas of impact and providing relevant examples and citations. The discussion of Large Language Models and Mass Online Deliberation is particularly relevant given current technological trends.
    *   **Forward-looking:** The discussion on the changing roles of representatives, specifically their potential role as architects of automated decision frameworks, is insightful and anticipates future challenges.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Depth:** As a review paper, it covers a wide range of topics but lacks in-depth analysis of specific areas. For example, the discussion of specific AI algorithms used in social media or the technical challenges of implementing MOD platforms could be more detailed.
    *   **Overreliance on Existing Literature:** The paper primarily synthesizes existing literature, and doesn't present any original empirical findings or theoretical frameworks. While this is expected of a review, it limits its novelty.
    *   **Somewhat General Recommendations:**  The conclusion advocates for hybrid systems that balance AI's benefits with democratic stability. However, the specific mechanisms or institutional designs for achieving this balance remain somewhat vague. It would benefit from concrete policy recommendations.
    *   **Future-oriented speculation:** While speculative, some sections of the paper lack strong evidence. More empirical data would be helpful.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   While the paper doesn't introduce entirely new concepts, it provides a valuable synthesis of the existing literature and identifies key challenges and opportunities related to AI and democratic representation. Its significance lies in bringing together various perspectives and highlighting the need for careful consideration of the ethical, political, and social implications of AI deployment in democratic systems. The novelty lies in its scope and ability to connect different areas.

*   **Justification for Score:**

    The paper is a solid overview of the landscape, but its reliance on synthesizing existing work limits its overall impact. The absence of any original data, concrete policy recommendations, or significant theoretical development prevents it from being considered truly groundbreaking. However, the comprehensive nature of the review, its balanced perspective, and its insightful observations about the future roles of representatives make it a worthwhile contribution to the field. The score takes into account its value as a starting point for future research and discussion, but acknowledges the need for more in-depth analysis and empirical validation in subsequent studies.
